# v1.6.0 Freight — work plan

**Commissioned:** 2026-08-18, on release of the 2.3 card. The tree is green:
`make -k check-source` runs to completion with zero failures and no exception
list, twice, the second time on a tree containing its own certificate.

**Freight order is frozen.** Cursor navigation leads, by standing owner
requirement.

**The train is not hostage to its wildcards.** Items 1 and 2 are committed
freight: v1.6.0 ships when they are green. Items 3 and 4 are **may-slip**
cards with their own halt — they ride along if they are ready and become
v1.7 freight if they are not. Neither may block the release of items 1 and 2,
and neither may be half-landed to make a date.

The reason is measured, not felt. The v1.5 era ran 2026-08-09 to 08-18 with
439 commits, of which 152 carry red-card, attribution or rescue language
against 95 that touch the freight itself: the cost was diagnosis, not
building. Items 1 and 2 are Bank-2 Lisp at zero resident cost — no linker,
no geometry, no capacity fight, which is where nearly every red card of that
era came from. Items 3 and 4 sit in exactly those areas.

## The agenda, in order

### 1. REPL cursor navigation (owner requirement, leads the train)

Today the line editor offers only destructive DEL to move back. Required:
non-destructive in-line navigation with insert-in-place, using **the
line-applicable subset of the IDE keymap** — Cursor Left/Right, `C-b`/`C-f`,
`C-a`/`C-e`, `Delete`/`C-d`; word motion and `C-k` priced separately.

The binding rule is single-source: both the editor and the REPL derive their
bindings from the one generated keymap table (`docs/generated/ide-keymap.md`,
41 bindings), with a parity gate so the two consumers can never drift. The
line editor is Bank-2 Lisp, so this is Bank-2 freight at zero resident cost,
priced against Bank-2 headroom and the D5 smoke bounds.

### 2. Comfort REPL (balanced input)

The owner's 2026-07-17 shelf-library design: evaluate only on balanced
parens via the 1.1-H scanner, Return inside an open form continues input, an
over-closed line is rejected *before* any form runs, auto-indent by scanner
depth, heap history, input beyond the 192-byte C-REPL line. Hard rules stand:
input layer only, identical eval path, the native C REPL remains the
boot/fail-closed fallback.

Depends on item 1: the comfort REPL reuses the navigation it introduces.

### 3. Attic write/readback boundary — **may slip to v1.7**

The last open member of the retired completion-trust class: role-4, ~3.7 s
timeout, 0/65,423 committed. Poison-first verification is already priced (516
DMA jobs, 130,846 comparisons). Enters as its own card with its own halt.

### 4. Startup levers, decided by measurement — **may slip to v1.7**

v1.5.0 ships at 36 s against v1.4.0's 31 s, documented openly. The parked
boot snapshot keeps that measurement as its baseline receipt. This slot
prices the lever ledger — snapshot, `require` bundling of the 612 index
reads, baked standard libraries against the 32/384 headroom contract, stager
and freelist batching — and commits only what the pricing supports. Target
sentence unchanged: under 30 s to prompt, `require` felt-instant.

## Halts

- **Halt A — committed freight.** Items 1 and 2 green on hardware. This halt
  decides the v1.6.0 release on its own; the answer may not be deferred for
  the may-slip cards.
- **Halt B — may-slip cards.** Taken separately for items 3 and 4, each on
  its own card. A card that is not green at Halt A becomes v1.7 freight by
  default, with no further argument required. The default direction is slip,
  not squeeze: carrying an unfinished wildcard is the failure mode this
  structure exists to prevent.

## Standing constraints

- No device contact without explicit owner authorization.
- Bound and frozen evidence stays immutable; provenance is read at its
  sealing era (`tools/host-lisp/evidence_era.py`).
- A closing commit is part of the tree it certifies: the proof run comes
  after the report is committed, not before. This cost the 2.3 card one
  reviewer round and is now house rule.
- Every card leaves a permanent gate with a mutation that proves it bites.

## Not in this train

The parity fork remains a separate owner decision. Interlisp-style editing
stays further out and stays **file-based** — the file-manager model, not a
residential image.


## Incident: a docs commit swept in-flight card work — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

Commit `f25daf65` carries this plan's may-slip edit **and, unintentionally,
a mid-flight snapshot of the cursor-navigation card**: the v1.6 keymap
contract, `lib/stdlib-read-line.lisp`, `config/v11-l-lite-keymap.json`, the
ship input-wait gate and its suite, a host-first receipt and a `mk/gates.mk`
edit. None of that is reviewer work. It was staged by a `git add -A` in the
shared working tree while the implementer was writing (contract 11:51,
read-line 12:09, commit 12:31, further gate edits 12:32).

Nothing was reverted: the implementer is mid-card, and rolling those paths
back would destroy live work to tidy a commit message. The snapshot is
therefore recorded here rather than erased, and the card's own commits
continue on top of it.

**Reviewer practice, effective immediately:** in a shared working tree, stage
explicit paths. `git add -A` is banned for the reviewer — it cannot tell
authorship from adjacency, and a commit message that misdescribes its own
contents is exactly the kind of record this project spends its effort
preventing.

Open for the owner: whether `main` should keep this mid-flight snapshot (it
is harmless and the card completes on top of it) or whether the card should
land as one clean commit series. Rolling `main` back would need a force push,
which is why it is an owner call and not a reviewer one.


## Item 1 accepted — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

Cursor navigation is host-green and independently verified. The reviewer
re-ran `make -k check-source` on the delivered tree including the closing
commit: exit 0, zero failed targets, 2,165 log lines, clean worktree
afterwards. The card applied the closing-commit house rule correctly.

Spot-verified beyond the report: insertion is genuine insertion at point --
`%rl-put` splices a new cell into the sentinel chain and grows the length, so
the tail is preserved rather than overwritten; there is no overwrite mode at
all, which is the right single behaviour for a REPL line. The generated
keymap block carries its BEGIN/END markers in `lib/stdlib-read-line.lisp`,
and the cursor gate is wired below the full 41-binding keymap check, so the
single-source claim reaches the real source.

### Condition on item 2: the symbol budget is priced first

**The interned-symbol margin is now exactly at the contractual floor: 32 free
slots against a minimum of 32.** v1.5.0 measured 34 on hardware; this card
projects 32. The name pool moved the other way and sits 178 bytes above its
floor.

Two things make that thin margin a gate rather than a note. The contract
declares `failure_is_release_terminal`, so a breach ends a release rather
than costing a revision. And 32 is a **projection from a host card**, while
the 34 it replaces was a hardware measurement at D5 — the number that decides
is the one taken on the device.

Comfort REPL is a shelf library and is the freight most likely to intern
public names. It therefore does not start until its symbol budget is priced,
naming which of these it relies on:

1. the comfort REPL interns **zero** additional symbols (cheapest, and
   plausible for an input-layer library that publishes little);
2. slots are **reclaimed** first, restoring a real margin before new freight
   lands;
3. `MAX_SYM` is **raised** — which costs resident Bank-0 bytes and would
   break the zero-resident-cost property that items 1 and 2 were commissioned
   with, so it needs the owner rather than the card.

A card that starts without that pricing is a card that discovers the wall at
D5, which is the most expensive place to find it.


## Symbol pricing reviewed — item 2 stays closed — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

The pricing card is mechanically green: the reviewer re-ran `make -k
check-source` on the delivered tree, exit 0, zero failed targets, clean
worktree. Its gate, its eleven mutations and its arithmetic are sound, and it
was right that a zero-symbol Comfort REPL is impossible because the native C
`read_line()` cannot be replaced by redefining the public Lisp function.

**But its load-bearing assumption is refuted by our own evidence.** The card
treats the post-cursor host projection as the conservative number and leaves
the device value to the release D5 row. The one time we can check that
assumption, it fails in the unsafe direction:

| Authority | Free symbol slots | Free name bytes |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| v1.5 host projection (`c2.3-v1.5.0-name-freight-implementation-receipt.json`) | 35 | 549 |
| v1.5 device measurement (`c2.3-v2.1-link116-name-freight-device-receipt.json`) | 34 | 545 |
| Measured projection bias | **−1** | **−4** |

The Link-116 device receipt already records this as
`pricing_projection_delta`. The host projection was **optimistic by one
symbol slot**. Applying the same bias to the priced default takes 32/565 to
**31/561 on the device** — below the floor, and the contract declares that
release-terminal.

So the selected path does not merely lack margin; on the only calibration we
possess it is projected to fail at the most expensive possible discovery
point.

### Standing rule from this finding

A capacity projection is compared against the floor **after** the measured
projection bias is applied, not before. A pricing that lands on the floor in
projected numbers has already failed. Concretely, until a better calibration
exists: **the host projection must be at least 33 free symbol slots** for a
release that must hold 32 on the device.

### What item 2 needs before it opens

The card returns with a pricing that clears the bias-adjusted floor. Three
routes exist and two of them are the owner's, exactly as the card said:

1. **Find one more slot** — reclaim a third private helper on the same
   single-call-site argument. Cheapest, changes no public surface, keeps the
   zero-resident property. This is the card's own route and needs no owner.
2. **One-name convergence** (owner) — `repl` as both designator and entry
   gives 33 projected, which is 32 on the device: a pass, but with no margin
   left over, and it drops the documented `repl-comfort` designator.
3. **Raise `MAX_SYM`** (owner) — restores real margin but costs at least four
   Bank-0 and 18 Bank-5 bytes and breaks the zero-resident-cost property that
   items 1 and 2 were commissioned with.

Route 1 combined with route 2 would give 34 projected and 33 on the device --
the first actual margin in this train.

The "no named private helper" rule the card imposes on the Comfort
implementation stays under review with it: it is a constraint born of a
capacity accident rather than of design, and a library that must carry a
paren scanner, auto-indent and a history ring without a single named helper
is likely to return for a new disposition anyway.


## Pricing accepted — item 2 opens — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

The bias-corrected pricing clears the rule this reviewer set. Verified
independently: `make -k check-source` on the delivered tree, exit 0, zero
failed targets, clean worktree.

| Step | Slots | Name bytes |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Post-cursor baseline | 32 | 562 |
| Reclaim `%rl-dispatch`, `%rl-put`, `%take` | 35 | 589 |
| Add `repl-comfort` and `repl` | 33 | 571 |
| **After the measured bias** | **32** | **567** |
| Release minimum | 32 | 384 |

The third reclaim is properly proven rather than asserted: `%take` is a
non-recursive wrapper with `butlast` as its only caller, and the receipt
carries a real compiler run over all 247 workbench cases showing
`helper_entry_absent` with `butlast` preserved. The transformation is priced,
not yet applied — the implementation applies it and re-proves it through the
accepted cursor gates.

**Item 2 is open on this budget.** Two things travel with it:

- **The slot margin at the device is zero**, and the receipt states so in its
  own numbers rather than rounding it away. The name pool keeps 183 bytes.
- **The first named private helper triggers a new pricing round.** That is
  the right structure — it makes an overspend impossible to commit silently —
  but it means the owner surface decision is one helper away. It is
  deliberately *not* pre-spent: `repl-comfort` keeps its documented
  designator until the implementation proves it needs the slot, and only then
  is the choice between one-name convergence (33 at the device) and
  `MAX_SYM+4` (34, at four Bank-0 and 24 Bank-5 bytes) worth making.

The earlier blanket ban on named Comfort helpers is lifted with this
acceptance, as the card proposed: a helper is now a pricing event, not a
design prohibition.


## Comfort REPL ships as a library; the default init defers to v1.7 — 2026-08-18 (owner)

The July design has two halves. v1.6 builds one of them.

**In v1.6:** the Comfort REPL is a **loadable shelf library**. The user
requires it and starts it with `(repl)`. The hard rules are unchanged: input
layer only, identical eval path, native C REPL remains the boot and
fail-closed path.

**Deferred to v1.7:** the batteries-included half — `INIT.L65` on the
writable work medium, auto-loaded at the end of boot, with the shipped
`L65WORK.D81` carrying a sensible default (m65d, ide, repl-comfort). That
mechanism does not exist today: neither `src/` nor `lib/` knows `INIT.L65`
and the user guide does not mention it. It is a boot hook plus a media
policy, which is its own freight and was never in this train's commission.

### Consequence the implementation must price, not assume

The accepted symbol pricing counts `repl-comfort` and `repl` as **standing
release surface**, which is what put us on the floor with zero slot margin.
That assumption came from the batteries-included model: a library the default
init always loads is effectively part of the shipped configuration.

With the default init deferred, that is no longer self-evident. A shelf
library that the shipped configuration does not load interns nothing until a
user requires it — in which case the two names may not belong in the D5
headroom row at all, and the margin we have been fighting for comes back on
its own.

It may equally stay true: if the release D5 row loads the Comfort library in
order to exercise it, its names are interned in the measured session and the
cost stands. That is a real question about what the headroom row measures,
and it decides whether this train has zero slots of margin or three.

**Codex prices it explicitly before implementation**, naming which
configuration the D5 headroom row measures. Neither answer may be assumed in
the implementation's favour; if the cost stands, the accepted budget stands
with it.


## Item 2 host implementation complete — 2026-08-18 (Codex)

The Comfort REPL is host-green as the owner-authorized shelf library. Its
activation remains explicit: require `repl-comfort`, then call `(repl)`; the
native C REPL remains the boot and fail-closed fallback, and `INIT.L65` remains
v1.7 freight.

The implementation required two named private helpers. The accepted cursor
helpers were not sacrificed to preserve the earlier forecast: eliminating
`%rl-put` either changed the public editor shape or exceeded the 255-byte code
object ceiling. The card instead proved and applies five genuine one-caller
private inlines (`%take`, `%case-fold-list`, `%fasl-len`, `%subseq-list`,
`%append2`) across all 248 Workbench cases. Four Comfort names are added, so
the final projection is 33/576 and the measured bias yields **32/572**: exact
slot floor, 188 name bytes of margin. D5 therefore loads the shelf before
measuring; no unloaded-library exemption is claimed.

Behavioral proof covers balanced and multiline input, sticky over-close
rejection before evaluation, two-space auto-indent, ten-line Up/Down history,
strings/comments, and a 229-byte heap aggregate built from short physical
lines. The emitted artifact is 529 code bytes plus 21 directory bytes over
three objects; the largest is 249 bytes. There are zero resident/native bytes,
zero product links and zero device contacts.

The full report is
`docs/planning/v1.6.0-comfort-repl-report.md`. The owner review decides the
single item-2 hardware acceptance contact; item 3 remains closed until that
review.

## Item 2 hardware First Red — input fidelity, 2026-08-18

The single contact passed all ten semantic rows and returned cleanly to the
native prompt, but it did not pass hardware acceptance. The owner reports that
Comfort typing is clunky relative to the native REPL and that physical
keystrokes are frequently lost and must be repeated. D5 and item 3 remain
closed.

The host-visible mechanism class is a slow synchronous Lisp consumer behind a
five-event producer queue. `key-event` materializes three cells and printable
insertion one more before the next poll; the 192-cell nursery therefore makes
collection a periodic part of the input hot path. The card's one-collection-
per-key ceiling was not a losslessness contract: the standing device price of
a whole collection is 88--89 frames, while the sixth queued event is discarded.

The next action is host-first pricing of a lossless capture boundary versus a
zero-allocation scalar-event/preallocated-editor path with an explicit queue
service bound. Queue-aware coalescing is admissible only if draining cannot
itself collect before the five hardware slots are empty. No further device
contact is authorized by this finding.


## Device first red accepted; the pricing round is commissioned — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

The first red (`0e027f9e`) is properly bound: 10/10 functional rows green,
the acceptance withheld on lost keystrokes, all further device contacts
closed. The attribution names a class, not an incident: a synchronous Lisp
input path behind a five-deep hardware queue, four heap cells per character,
a long collection due by roughly 48 characters — during which the sixth
waiting event is dropped. The native REPL bypasses that path, which explains
the felt difference.

**Lesson bound against the host card:** its allocation gate priced cells and
collections per key, not drain latency. Functional host green plus a cell
budget does not witness real-time loss; only the device does. Future input
freight carries a loss model (queue depth × worst-case pause × typing rate),
not only a cell count.

**Commissioned: one host-only pricing round**, zero device contacts, both
candidates priced against each other:

1. **Lossless capture path** — drain the hardware queue ahead of the Lisp
   path (IRQ-side or polled ring). Must name its resident cost in bytes; any
   resident cost breaks the zero-resident property of this train and is
   therefore an owner decision, not a card decision.
2. **Allocation-free event/editor path** — no allocation on the per-key hot
   path, so no collection can interpose. The price must cover the **whole**
   path including `key-event`'s own three cells, not only the editor's part;
   an "allocation-free editor" behind an allocating event primitive has not
   removed the class.

Both prices carry the same acceptance test: the class witness is typing
faster than the queue drains across a forced long collection — the exact
scenario the first red bound. A fix that cannot state why the sixth event
survives is not a fix. Hybrid pricing (small capture + reduced allocation) is
in scope if it dominates both.

D5, item 3 and all further device contacts stay closed until the owner
releases the priced fix.


## Item 2 input-fidelity pricing complete — 2026-08-18

The common forced-collection test decides the architecture before code style
does. Five already-pending hardware events plus one physical event per frame
through the accepted 89-frame collection require storage for **94 events**.
A synchronous zero-allocation editor still loses event six during that forced
pause, so allocation elimination alone is not a fix.

The selected price is the smallest passing hybrid: the owned raster IRQ drains
raw PETSCII code bytes into a 112-slot ring (111 usable), and Comfort consumes
those scalar bytes without the three-cell `key-event` tuple while retaining
the existing one-cell insertion. The executable model captures 94/94 in order,
including event six, with 17-event margin. Printable hot-path allocation falls
from four cells to one, raising the no-other-allocation collection interval
from 48 to 192 keys.

The price is exact and **breaks the zero-resident promise**:

- 17-byte IRQ wrapper + 52-byte queue drain = **69 mapped C2-window bytes**;
- **5 ordinary Bank-0 text bytes** disable capture on native error recovery;
- two already-reserved fixed-state bytes (`$FF8C/$FF8D`) gain owners;
- 112 existing Bank-0 bytes in `repl.buf` (`$BC90..$BCFF`) are lifetime-aliased
  exclusively while Comfort owns input;
- no state section grows, but total new emitted resident code is **74 bytes**;
- Bank-2 bytecode grows 251 bytes across three existing objects, with no new
  symbol or primitive and a 237-byte maximum object under the 255-byte wall.

After the price, ordinary text retains 6 bytes and the mapped C2-window gap
retains 537. Full event tuples do not fit reused storage (91 usable versus 94
required); a fully preallocated editor pays the same mandatory capture plus a
non-local refactor and supplies no additional losslessness.

**Owner decision required:** authorize or reject the 74 resident code bytes,
the two fixed-state owners and the 112-byte exclusive lifetime alias. This
pricing round authorizes no implementation, product link, medium or hardware.
D5, item 3 and all device contacts remain closed.

Full derivation: [v1.6.0-comfort-input-fidelity-pricing-report.md](v1.6.0-comfort-input-fidelity-pricing-report.md).


## Owner release: the resident price is accepted — 2026-08-18

The owner releases the hybrid ("gebe frei", 2026-08-18): the lossless capture
boundary may be implemented at its priced cost — +69 bytes mapped C2-window
code, +5 bytes ordinary Bank-0 text, two fixed-state owners
(`$FF8C/$FF8D`), the 112-byte Comfort-lifetime alias of the `repl.buf` tail,
and +251 Bank-2 bytecode bytes. The zero-resident property of this train ends
here, knowingly: after the `$A0` era, input fidelity is a core product
property, and the priced alternative fails the acceptance test structurally
rather than by cost.

The implementation card inherits the walls bound in
`docs/planning/v1.6.0-comfort-input-fidelity-pricing-report.md` unchanged:
the 94-event forced-collection test with event six observed in order, ring
full/empty and commit-order proofs, capture disabled before evaluation and on
every exit path, `$A0 → $20` at the raw consumer, matrix RUN/STOP authority,
public `key-event` and native-REPL fallback untouched, and the resident
deltas carried honestly.

**Watch item (reviewer, register-bound):** ordinary Bank-0 text falls to
**6 free bytes** — the thinnest number in the product. The next freight that
needs ordinary text meets this wall first and must say so before it links.

Device contacts stay closed until the implementation card is host-green and
review-green; the repeated device acceptance of items 1 and 2 then follows.


## Item 2 implementation First Red — the priced C2 gap was an interval, not capacity — 2026-08-18

The implementation preflight stopped before changing product source.  The
released 69-byte capture does not fit the bound candidate's delivered C2
window.  The pricing called `$FD22..$FF80` a 606-byte gap, but that interval
already contains 348 bytes of profile rodata and the 223-byte `reopen_gap1`
tenant.  Only 35 bytes of that claimed interval are free.

Enumerating every allocated section in the complete `$E000..$FF80` arena
finds two holes: 36 bytes at `$FD08..$FD2B` and 25 bytes at
`$FF67..$FF7F`.  Their aggregate is **61 bytes**, eight short of the accepted
69-byte wrapper plus drain, and the 52-byte drain does not fit either hole
contiguously.  The pricing gate passed because it compared the capture against
the literal 606-byte contract value without enumerating the linked ELF's
tenants.

This is a placement First Red, not a rejection of the loss model or hybrid
architecture.  The 94-event contract, ordinary 5-byte cleanup, fixed-state
owners, buffer lifetime alias, public ABI and input semantics remain standing.
Before implementation can continue, a new price must either reclaim/relocate
at least eight aggregate C2 bytes while providing a contiguous home for the
52-byte drain, or redesign capture below the real 61-byte aggregate ceiling.
No product source, product link, medium or device was touched.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-comfort-input-fidelity-placement-first-red-receipt.json`.


## Rescue authorized: the 59-byte split seam — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

The placement first red (`912ddf23`) is accepted as attributed: the pricing
did range arithmetic on `$FD22..$FF80` and called 606 bytes free while the
linked image already carried 348 bytes of profile rodata and 223 bytes of
reopen freight there. Truly free: 36 bytes at `$FD08` and 25 at `$FF67` — 61
against the released 69. Not implementing silently under the old release was
the right call.

**The 59-byte split seam is authorized under the reviewer's delegation**: it
costs strictly less than the owner-released price (59 C2 bytes ≤ 69; ordinary
+5 and Bank-2 +251 unchanged), and the architecture, loss model and walls are
untouched — the first red itself limits its claim to placement.

Conditions, each a gate rather than a sentence:

1. **Placement is born-derived.** The implementation's placement gate reads
   both gaps from ElfTruth on the final linked ELF and proves each fragment
   sits inside its gap; range arithmetic on assumed-free spans is rejected by
   a mutation. The class rule extends explicitly: **free space is a derived
   quantity of the linked image, never of address arithmetic** — this red is
   the born-derived clause violated for space instead of values.
2. **The STZ→JSR substitution is proven, not narrated.** The IRQ handler's
   byte count is unchanged; the displaced STZ executes in the main body on
   every path on which the original executed, including early exits — shown
   against the emitted bytes.
3. **All released walls carry over unchanged**, the 94-event
   forced-collection test first among them; the split (helper in the second
   gap) must show its call linkage in the placement gate.

**Register update:** the C2-window reserve of **2 bytes** joins the
capacity watch row — the product's two thinnest numbers are now 2 bytes
(C2 window) and 6 bytes (ordinary text). Anything that wants either names
the wall before it links.


## Item 2 implementation product-card First Red — candidate plane not projected — 2026-08-18

The 59-byte implementation is host-preflight green: the target assembler
emits 34-byte main and 25-byte helper fragments, the IRQ handler remains 74
bytes, the fixed-state section remains 16 bytes, the Bank-2 delta is 251
bytes, and the executable forced-collection model preserves all 94 events in
order including event six. No wall from the release or split authorization
failed.

The one product card stopped **before WPLTO and before a product link**. Its
Link-116-derived producer adapter emitted the historical six-image input world
with 43,308 static Bank-2 code bytes, while the current candidate header
correctly requires 46,043. The 2,735-byte deficit is therefore a producer
configuration red: the wrapper inherited the final-link adapter but did not
run the candidate's complete static-plane/configurator projection before the
real consumer. The compiler refused the mismatch; no PRG or ELF exists.

This is another concrete member of **bound/configured is not consumed**. A
replacement may run only after the product card's preflight executes the real
candidate six-image configurator closure and proves the 46,043-byte value at
the consuming `static_plane()` call. The consumed card is not replayable and
its Final Red explicitly carries `retry_authorized=false`; a replacement card

## The park stands; the train is at an owner fork — 2026-08-18 (reviewer)

The fourth red (`f5ad67fc`) is a genuine product finding, not card
mechanics: the linker's own pinned floor requires 54 free bytes in the E000
arena, the arena holds 61, and the 59-byte seam would leave 2. The true
budget for any capture in that arena was therefore **7 bytes**, and no form
of this seam fits it. The placement pricing was refuted twice over — range
arithmetic first (606 → 61), the floor second (61 → 7). The seam is parked
exactly as the end edge bound, with three permanent gates left behind
(consumer bytes, closure ordering, execute-once selector) and the product
untouched.

**Consequence for the train, stated plainly:** without the capture, the
device acceptance of items 1 and 2 stays red on lost keystrokes — the
committed freight of this train is host-proven and device-blocked. Reopening
the seam requires roughly 52+ bytes freed in the E000/C2 arena: an
architecture block, not a card.

The reviewer recommends: commission a **host-only E000 relocation study** —
born-derived from ElfTruth, priced, zero device contacts — that answers
whether ≥52 bytes of current E000/C2 tenants can move (to Bank 2 or
elsewhere) without breaking their mapped-access reasons. Halt A holds until
it reports. The alternative is descoping the train (items 1 and 2 become
v1.7 freight behind the relocation); that choice is the owner's.


## E000 relocation study commissioned — 2026-08-18 (owner)

The owner approves the recommended path ("Ich stimme zu"). Commissioned: a
**host-only E000 relocation study**, zero device contacts, no product source
changes, no links — a study, not a card.

**Question it must answer, with numbers:** can at least 52 bytes of current
E000/C2-arena tenants move elsewhere (Bank 2 first) without breaking the
reason each tenant is mapped where it is? The parked capture seam needs
59 bytes over a 54-byte floor in an arena holding 61.

Ground rules:

1. **Born-derived throughout.** Every tenant, size and address comes from
   ElfTruth on the bound candidate ELF; range arithmetic is disqualified by
   the standing rule this saga bought.
2. **Per-tenant verdict.** For each E000/C2 tenant: bytes, why it is mapped
   (IRQ reachability, KERNAL window choreography, timing), whether it can
   move, where to, and what the move costs (call-path changes, Bank-2
   bytecode delta, any new resident bytes). "Cannot move" is a finding with
   a reason, not a shrug.
3. **The floor itself is in scope as a question, not a target.** If the
   54-byte floor guards something measurable, the study names it; if its
   size is historical, the study says what evidence would justify changing
   it. No floor change is proposed as part of the relocation arithmetic.
4. **Output:** a priced relocation plan reaching ≥52 freed bytes (or the
   proof that none exists), each step with its own acceptance gate, ordered
   so the seam's reopen is a card again rather than an architecture bet.

Halt A holds until the study reports. The parked seam's three permanent
gates and the loss-model rule stay binding for whatever reopens it.

## Second replacement R1 Final Red — activation syntax was not consumed — 2026-08-19

The one authorized second replacement R1 is consumed, and the R1 family
returns to the owner as bound. Its preflight was green without a WPLTO or
link: the stored 1,248-byte inventory value was replaced by a
candidate-derived section-size policy bounded by the 1,499-byte arena, R1's
configuration carried no `LISP65_V160_INPUT_CAPTURE`, and both the activation
and predecessor-pin mutations fell.

The real assembler consumer then exposed the boundary defect before emitting
a seed or product ELF/PRG. `src/c2_kernal_window.s` is a lower-case `.s`
assembler input. The new `#ifdef LISP65_V160_INPUT_CAPTURE` lines therefore
did not select one body in that real path: the emitted object contains both
the 34-byte capture main and 25-byte capture helper even though R1 left the
define unset. The same object emitted 32 state bytes where the linker contract
requires 16, proving that both conditional state branches were assembled as
well. The seed linker correctly refused with `C2 KERNAL-window state geometry
drift` and orphan capture-section overflows.

This is the precise failure of the newly required rule: the activation was
bound in producer configuration and modeled by the linker generator, but was
not consumed as conditional syntax by the real assembler input. It is not a
third objection to the abort relocation itself; the card never produced the
ELF required to rerun its 9-byte-entry, 98-byte-facade, worst-state, ABI and
unmap proofs. It is nevertheless the third R1-family Red. Per the explicit
delegation boundary there is no syntax conversion, source split, retry,
Completion, media, or device action here. R1 and the input-fidelity reopen
remain parked for owner disposition.

## Zero-literal witness attribution commissioned — 2026-08-19 (owner)

The owner follows the review recommendation: a **host-only attribution**, not
a card. Zero device contacts, no product change, no link.

The fifth R1-family red (`8a35ad4a`) reached WPLTO and the product link for
the first time — every mechanics class of the family is closed — and stopped
in the historical C2D zero-literal consumer with a bound drift and **no bound
cause**: the gate persisted only the actual line and the deviation, never its
computed expectation. The attribution therefore:

1. reconstructs the witness's expected line and names, from linked evidence,
   exactly what the witness measures and which input moved it;
2. decides mechanically between the two hypotheses — a stored-world witness
   that R1's relocation legitimately moves (then the successor card is a
   conversion under the standing derived-not-pinned rule), or a genuine
   zero-literal regression caused by the relocation (then the successor is a
   fix and R1's semantic proofs are re-examined, not assumed);
3. repairs the gate as a permanent rule: **a gate that reports drift
   persists both sides** — its computed expectation next to the observed
   line. A drift report that cannot show what it expected forces exactly the
   guessing this attribution exists to avoid;
4. adopts no mechanism text it has not proven — the claim-hygiene line the
   red itself already held.

No successor card is authorized until the attribution reports.

## Zero-literal witness attribution — stored-world consumer — 2026-08-19

The commissioned host-only attribution is complete. It ran no WPLTO, product
link, media build, or device contact and changed no product source.

The rejected comparison is reconstructed on both sides. The gate derived the
semantic `%lcc-consp` witness from a six-role inventory whose first role was
the locally emitted 344-entry `stdlib-p0`. In that world the witness is global
ordinal 609 and its expected row is:

`0500fd8b2600f5080100`

It then indexed a C2D emitted from the bound product inventory, whose stdlib
has 393 entries. Row 609 in that C2D is the unrelated m65d row the Final Red
persisted:

`030101845400cb080100`

The two inventories differ in exactly one input: `stdlib-p0`, by exactly 49
entries. The other five manifest SHAs are identical. In the bound product
world `%lcc-consp` therefore lives at ordinal 658, exactly 49 positions later,
with row:

`0500b49626007c090100`

That row is present byte-for-byte in the linked C2D. Its code offset `$96b4`
selects exactly 38 bytes from the linked 46,043-byte static-code image, and
those bytes equal the canonical `%lcc-consp` code witness. All five R1-family
attempts, including the pre-relocation and equate-owner worlds, carry the
same C2D SHA `d576a0ff...b6043c19`. R1 did not move or regress the C2D.

**Decision: stored-world/cross-inventory witness, not a zero-literal
regression.** The successor shape is a Derived-not-pinned consumer conversion:
the witness identity and its row/ordinal must be derived from the same bound
C2D/product-manifest set the gate reads, never from a second live inventory.
This attribution does not authorize that successor card.

The reporting defect is repaired independently in the host gate. Every
linked-witness refusal now renders and therefore persists all three fields:
ordinal, computed expected row, and observed row. Three mutations remove one
field apiece and all fall. Successful receipts likewise record distinct
`expected_row_hex` and `observed_row_hex` fields. This is reporting and source
qualification only; it does not convert the consumer's authority under this
commission.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-zero-literal-witness-attribution-receipt.json`.

## Standing delegation: review decisions no longer route through the owner — 2026-08-19 (owner)

The owner binds a new working rule: **future review decisions belong to the
reviewer without owner coordination.** Card dispositions, replacement
authorizations, attributions, conversions, gate and preflight rulings — the
entire loop that has been running through owner confirmations — now closes
at the review desk directly.

What stays with the owner, by nature rather than by this rule:

- **physical device contacts** — the owner sits at the machine; every
  contact remains an owner act and is announced as before;
- **the release words** (Ship / Publish) and everything the release law
  already reserves to the owner;
- **owner-bound product promises** — public-surface changes (e.g. the
  one-name convergence), resident-cost commitments beyond an already
  released price, and capacity-floor changes. These are announced with a
  recommendation, as before, unless the owner delegates them too.

The reviewer reports outcomes and findings as before; only the
authorization round-trips end.

## Standing delegation for the implementer: bounded self-disposition — 2026-08-19 (owner)

The owner approves the reviewer's proposal ("ja"). Effective immediately:

**Self-disposition.** When a red matches an already-gated class family with
a standing rule — derived-not-stored conversions, structural-enumeration
registration, the real-caller rung (signatures and vocabulary), setup/owner
scope — the implementer may authorize and run the replacement card
**itself**, provided the fix is the minimal named conversion under the
standing rule. The disposition is bound in the plan exactly as before; the
audit trail is unchanged, and the review desk reads it afterwards instead
of ahead.

**Budget.** At most one replacement per red, and at most **three
consecutive self-dispositions per seam**; the fourth red of a seam comes to
the review desk with the full chain, so an oscillating chain never runs
autonomously.

**Hard stops, unchanged:** new classes without a standing rule;
anything touching product behavior, walls, capacity, floors or prices;
attributions with an open outcome; golden questions; device contacts;
releases. These carried the historical misclasses and keep their
threshold.

The rationale is the ledger of this saga: roughly seventeen reds since the
input-fidelity first red cost almost nothing — zero device contacts, almost
no wasted WPLTO — because the stop discipline turned each into a permanent
gate. The stops that still check something stay; the ones that had become
pure confirmation end.

once and wrong the second time because it ran outside its phase.

**Authorized: exactly one replacement card** with one change, under a rule
now named permanently: **a guard belongs to the phase whose invariant it
protects.** The root-absence guard runs exactly once, pre-invocation, in
the producer phase. The read-only scope phase asserts the opposite and
correct invariant: the root **exists and is SHA-bound** to the production
receipt. Mutations, both permanent: a pre-production guard firing in a
read-only phase falls; a scope phase missing the identity assertion falls.

Everything else unchanged; exceptionless. Green reaches scope and
acceptance — the last two rungs before the device path.

## Companion-without-trigger attribution — exact writer and known class — 2026-08-19

The commissioned host-only attribution reports against the frozen fourth-Red
pair without a link, card, media build, or device contact.  Both frozen
artifacts remain SHA-identical before and after the raw-first reproduction.

The two persisted sides are now exact.  The real Scope consumer selects only
`LISP65_V160_INPUT_CAPTURE`.  Its rightful source-owner row is
`v160-input-capture`, whose trigger and sole define are that same identity.
The observed `mapped-far-content-convergence` row nevertheless contains
`LISP65_V160_INPUT_CAPTURE` while its own trigger
`LISP65_CODE_WINDOW_CONVERGENCE` is absent from the consumer input.  The guard
therefore rejected a real companion-without-trigger state.

The exact writer chain is mechanical:

1. `configure_input_capture()` at
   `tools/host-lisp/c2_product_substitution_link.py:1289` adds the capture
   identity to the global `CONVERGENCE_DEFINES` build aggregate.  At this
   point the dedicated capture owner row is still correct and the mapped-far
   row is still clean.
2. `configure_fix_source()` at
   `tools/host-lisp/c2_v20_map_tuple_fix_card.py:101` is the exact scope
   writer: it first constructs the mapped-far replacement row with
   `defines = PRODUCT.CONVERGENCE_DEFINES`, then installs that row.  Thus it
   copies a cross-owner global aggregate wholesale into one local owner
   domain.  Later full-span, CPU-read and R1 layers propagate the already
   misclassified membership; they did not create it.

**Classification: known domain-membership family, not a product finding and
not a new class.**  This is the established right-value/wrong-domain rule in
registry form: **a definition belongs only to the source-owner row whose
identity declares it; global build membership is never owner-scope
membership.**  The required successor form is therefore a self-dispositional
conversion that derives each row from its owner identity instead of copying
`PRODUCT.CONVERGENCE_DEFINES`.  Per the commissioning decision, this completed
attribution resets the seam's self-disposition budget.  This report authorizes
no successor card; it precedes one.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-input-fidelity-companion-trigger-attribution.json`.

## Placement-escape attribution — dichotomy refuted by zero-byte freight — 2026-08-19 (implementer)

The commissioned host-only attribution completed without configuration,
qualification, WPLTO, link, card, media, or device work.  It persisted both
requested geometries and found that they agree exactly:

- the current failed-link map derives the main hole as `$FD08..$FD2B`
  (36 bytes) and the helper hole as `$FEE1..$FF7F` (159 bytes);
- the generated linker assigns main to `$FD08` and helper to `$FEE1`, in both
  cases by predecessor-end expressions (`ADDR(reopen_gapN) + SIZEOF(...)`),
  not by numeric pricing-era addresses; and
- 34+25 payload bytes would leave 2+134 = **136 bytes** free, exactly the
  post-R1 price.  No hole shrank and no re-pricing is indicated.

The assertion messages hid a third mechanism.  Both capture output sections
exist in the failed map with **size zero**.  The assertion's `SIZEOF == 34/25`
conjunct failed before placement could say anything about fit.  The real
compiler receipt selected `src/c2_kernal_irq_base.s`; it contains neither
`LISP65_V160_INPUT_CAPTURE` in `feature_defines` nor an input identity for
`src/optional/c2_kernal_input_capture.s`.  The canonical object inventory
independently contains `052-c2_kernal_irq_base.s.o` and no capture-owner
object.  As a control, the last linked capture-world ELF carries the expected
34 bytes at `$FD08` and 25 bytes at `$FEE1`.

**Classification:** neither commissioned branch.  This is
`bound-layout-with-unconsumed-source-owner`: layout activation survived into
the linker generator, while the real compiler's `probe_definitions` selected
the base IRQ owner.  It is an instance of the known principle *bound is not
consumed*, but it is a new mechanism relative to the two expressly authorized
successor branches.  Therefore no self-disposition, fix, retry, or successor
card is taken.  The attribution returns to review with successor count zero.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-input-fidelity-placement-escape-attribution.json`.

The accepted Golden correctly describes its 103-section world, while the

authorized, candidate-derived sections.  Resolution changes an acceptance
authority and is therefore a review decision.  The two legal review forms are

## Acceptance authority decided: v5 stands; card freight validates additively — 2026-08-19 (reviewer)

boundaries, eleven arenas). Product, placement and capacity are exonerated.

changed:** v5 remains the unchanged geometry authority. Acceptance
validates the two sections as **additive, candidate-derived card freight**

by `db9752ac`), placement from the derived placement gates. This extends

placement is already derived from predecessor ends and separately gated.

2. **no double authority:** a golden-covered section offered as card

**Authorized: the acceptance resume**, read-only over the frozen pair of
the consumed card (the R1 closure pattern; no new WPLTO, link or card).
Green closes the input-fidelity seam and re-opens the device path.

## Tenth drift disposed: the housekeeping receipt splits into witness and derivation — 2026-08-19 (reviewer)

move re-stales it and buys another `--write` — the 2.2 block itself paid
that toll repeatedly. The same structural rule that ended the rebind
treadmill applies:

**Authorized: one repair** (host-only, checker/receipt only, no product):

1. the receipt **splits along the witness line**. Rows that witness what
   the v1.2 housekeeping actually did remain sealed, era-bound at their
   sealing commit — a historical record, never regenerated again;
2. rows that describe the living repository (counters, living-file SHAs,
   inventory aggregates) are **derived at check time and never persisted**
   — there is nothing left to go stale and nothing left to regenerate;
3. mutations, permanent: a living value persisted back into the receipt
   falls; a sealed witness row modified falls; the `--write` path for
   living rows is removed, and invoking it falls.

This ends the `--write` treadmill for good and removes the standing
idempotence caveat this receipt contributed to `check-source`. After the
repair, the full `make -k check-source` run decides whether the ten drifts
were the whole tail; the device contact stays closed until that run is
green.

## Owner testimony reframes the sluggishness class — 2026-08-19

First-class owner evidence, recorded per the house rule the `$A0` era
bound:

1. the pre-capture editor was **not** felt as direct — it was already
   somewhat laggy; the current candidate is much worse;
2. the loss did **not** occur roughly every 48 characters but roughly
   **every 5** — the depth of the hardware queue.

This partially refutes the bound first-red mechanism. Loss at queue depth
under ordinary typing means the queue filled in **steady state**: the Lisp
editor path's per-character cost already exceeded the typing rate, and the
long collection was an amplifier, not the cause. The capture ring therefore
converted losses into visible latency (characters queue instead of dying)
and its consumer added further per-character cost on top.

**Consequences for the open attribution, bound now:**

1. the rescue read keeps its purpose (accumulating ring vs render/GC
   stall), and gains a second question: the steady-state numbers — ring
   fill level against input cadence;
2. the attribution must produce the number the era never measured:
   **frames per character, end to end, on the real consumer path** — for
   the old key-event path and the ring path both, against a stated typing
   cadence budget;
3. the acceptance bar for any successor is a **responsiveness row**:
   consumer rate ≥ producer rate with named margin in steady state, plus
   the existing loss wall across a forced collection. Losslessness without
   drain rate is bookkeeping, not usability — the owner's testimony is the
   witness.

The reviewer's earlier "direct and snappy" characterization of the
pre-capture editor is withdrawn as unevidenced.

## Service-time pricing result — 2026-08-19

The commissioned host-only measurement is complete. The exact Workbench P0
path measures 1,727 VM steps/character through historical `key-event` and
1,776 through the raw ring: the scalar dequeue can recover only **49 steps**.
It is therefore rejected as a standalone fix. Even after direct state-tail
access and printable-first dispatch, scalar service prices at 1.576 calibrated
frames/character and remains above the 0.8-frame responsiveness ceiling.

The first passing point is an **adaptive eight-event hybrid**: native scalar
dequeue plus one shared PETSCII normalization authority, followed by batched
printable edit/state publication/dirty-span rendering. It measures 219.875 VM
steps and 1.125 screen cells per character, or **0.768 calibrated
frames/character = 1.302 events/frame** after the standing one-cell GC price.
That clears the one-event/frame producer by 30.2%, above the named 25% margin;
batch four remains red at 0.893 frames/character.

The freight is also priced: the touched Bank-2 objects shrink by 143 bytes,
there are zero new interned names and zero new public primitives, and the
assembled native body is 70 E000 bytes. The post-capture arena has 82 bytes
above its unchanged floor, leaving a hard 12-byte adapter allowance. Ordinary
text remains a non-budget at 6 free bytes. A successor must prove the complete
70+adapter seam from the final ELF without ordinary-text growth or return for
relocation.

The uppercase First Red rides with the successor: one generated normalization
table, two consumers, parity over all 256 **raw PETSCII** inputs; pre-normalized
ASCII fixtures are invalid. The responsiveness row, 94-event forced-collection
loss wall, WYSIWYG and RUN/STOP semantics all remain mandatory. No card or
device contact is authorized by this study.

## EACCES attribution result — 2026-08-20

The commissioned premise is corrected by the persisted command roles: the
JSON pathname was never in the executable position.  The consumer expected
and received Python, `c2_asm_leaf_abi_gate.py`, the frozen candidate ELF and
the JSON output pathname in their proper four roles.  The pathname in the
`EACCES` diagnostic is the target of `audit_elf()`'s `Path.write_text()`, not
an attempted executable.

The exact mechanism is a phase-owned-output violation.  The card runs scope
and acceptance, then schedules the ABI qualification report beneath the
producer-owned `wplto` root.  At that point the root is correctly sealed
`0555` and its artifacts `0444`.  The same real ABI gate over the same frozen
ELF passes when its report is directed to a writable qualification-owned
temporary directory.  Product and ABI semantics are therefore exonerated;
argument ordering, adapter projection and configuration role inversion are
all mechanically refuted.

**Decision-tree result: known family.**  This is the standing phase/output
ownership rule: a phase writes only its own outputs, and read-only
qualification never writes below a producer-owned frozen root.  The
self-disposition budget resets as commissioned.  This attribution authorizes
no card or resume itself; the known-family successor may now be bound under
the standing delegation.  Frozen ELF, PRG and Final-Red receipt remained
SHA-identical, with zero WPLTOs, links, cards, media builds or device contacts.

## Consumer-absence attribution — real compiler world omitted the hybrid — 2026-08-20

The frozen accepted pair decides the world question without inference.  Its
final ELF (`6832bb95…`) has no

The exact split is configuration order.  The hybrid wrapper calls the
inherited `configure_stack()` through `core.install()` first and invokes
`configure_input_hybrid()` only after that call returns.  The later fresh
profile probe and post-configuration owner registry see the mutation; the
already-installed compiler world does not.  Thus the receipt juxtaposed
three different claims: a 67-byte object compiled from the consumer source
alone, a fresh synthetic profile containing the hybrid feature, and the
actual final ELF in which `hybrid_consumer_present` was false.  Only the
third describes the shipped candidate.

The false value stayed green because the placement reader treated it as
telemetry.  The card required the standalone object price and the fresh
profile probe, but neither the card nor Acceptance asserted that the final
ELF's recorded presence predicate was true.  The permanent rule is now
executable: **a receipt may not record a claim-relevant value it does not
enforce.**  Mutations for recorded-false, omitted predicate, and substitution
of standalone-object proof for linked presence all fail.

The independent review missed the defect for the same structural reason:
it re-ran the reported gates in situ, but no gate joined the producing
compiler profile, canonical object closure and final linked section.  It
therefore confirmed component behavior and a synthetic configuration, not
final-product membership.  The Hybrid's performance, normalization and loss
claims must be re-verified by a successor; they are not inherited from this
false-green card.

The host-only attribution is sealed in
`c2.3-v1.6-hybrid-consumer-absence-attribution.json`.  It authorizes no fix,
WPLTO, link, media or device contact.  The quarantined media preparation
remains unpublished, and no successor exists until separately dispositioned.

## IRQ acknowledge-choreography attribution commissioned — 2026-08-20 (reviewer)

The rescue read at the stopped hybrid contact names the mechanism cleanly
and exonerates the new freight's data path: ring logically empty, no GC
stall, no OOM, MAP clean, hybrid consumer and rendering uninvolved in this
red. The frame is the fail-closed loop (`$E096`) reached because
`C2K_SOURCELESS_IRQS` saw a **second consecutive IRQ without a recognized
raster source** after Comfort activation, with the interrupted return
sitting in ordinary product code (`buf_from_string+$09`). The episode
guard worked as designed; the question is why activation produces that
signature.

**Commissioned: a host-only attribution**, evidence base the frozen
`capture.json` and the emitted bytes of the final ELF. It reconstructs the
capture wrapper's complete IRQ choreography — entry, hardware-queue drain,
**raster acknowledge order**, exit — against the episode logic's
source-recognition window, and decides mechanically between the two
candidate classes:

1. **acknowledge-order defect in the wrapper** (the ack lands where the
   next IRQ presents source-less — the standing ordering family, one
   more time at the hardware boundary), or
2. **episode-logic misclassification** (the counter counts the wrapper's
   own owned IRQ as source-less because the recognition window predates
   the hybrid's entry path).

Both sides persisted from bytes, not prose; no unproven mechanism text.
The known third possibility — a genuine second IRQ source armed by
activation — is ruled in or out by the same reconstruction. No resume, no
reset, no further contact; the device may stay off until a repaired
candidate is host- and review-green.


## IRQ attribution reports BRK, not IRQ — 2026-08-20 (Codex)

The frozen row corrects the commission's premise before choosing between
its IRQ branches.  The handler frame above `SP=$01c5` is
`00 c7 00 00 32 b8 c5`: saved Z/Y/X/A followed by stacked `P=$32` and
continuation `$c5b8`.  Bit 4 of stacked P is therefore one.  Under the
already tested and bound core authority, hardware IRQ stacks B=0 and its
resume PC, while software BRK stacks B=1 and the PC two bytes after opcode
`$00`.  The terminal entry was **BRK at CPU-view `$c5b6`**, not a second
hardware IRQ.

That changes the meaning of the otherwise correct latch observation.
`C2K_SOURCELESS_IRQS=$01` is shared by source-less hardware IRQ and BRK
vector ingress; it proves that the fail-closed edge saw a second member of
that class without an intervening owned raster, but it does not identify
the second member as hardware IRQ.  The original report's phrase "two
source-less IRQs" is withdrawn and replaced by "a terminal BRK entered an
already-set source-less/BRK episode".

The emitted IRQ choreography independently exonerates the two commissioned
wrapper hypotheses for the terminal entry.  The linked handler at
`$e038..$e081` classifies `D019&1` before any Hybrid call, acknowledges an
owned raster at `$e045`, and only then calls the capture body at `$fd08`.
The body first clears the episode latch and never writes D019.  A raster
that becomes pending during queue drain therefore remains visible at the
next entry; the wrapper cannot turn that owned raster into the captured
B=1 frame.  Stacked B=1 also excludes every genuine hardware IRQ source.

The continuation exposes the next boundary without overclaiming it.  In the
candidate ELF, the intended owner is `buf_from_string` at `$c5af`, and its
byte at the inferred BRK site `$c5b6` is `$02`, not `$00` (neighborhood
`69 f8 85 02 a5 03 69`).  Thus the executing CPU view did not match that
intended candidate owner.  The authorized row did not read live CPU-view
`$c5b6`, so this attribution does **not** name which overlay-install or
control-transfer mechanism supplied the zero byte.  That mechanism remains
open and no fix or successor card follows from this report.

control position is inconsistent with the intended instruction stream too.

## Two-track report: primary fault named; holder read ready — 2026-08-20 (Codex)

**Track 1 reproduces against the delivered Comfort blob.**  The minimal
product-shaped sequence is `(repl)` with empty history, Cursor Down (`$11`),
then an ordinary interval in which no further key has arrived.  After the
cursor command is consumed and rejected as history navigation because the
history is empty, `%read-line-loop` polls again.  Its Comfort path reaches
private `key-event` mode 2 through `%rl-render` with row `-1`.  That mode is a
nonblocking scalar dequeue and correctly returns `NIL` for the empty ring.
The legacy adapter then computes `(cadr event)` for that `NIL` and feeds the
result to **`(>= code 32)`**.  The comparison expects a fixnum key code and
receives `NIL`: this is the primary `VM_TYPE`.

The artifact proof uses the shipped `repl-comfort.blob.bin` and its actual
resident suite.  Cursor Down followed by a real empty interval reproduces
`TypeError` in 4,196 VM steps.  The control with Cursor Down and Return both
preloaded passes in 4,642 steps.  That control explains the former false
green: the fixtures preloaded all later events and therefore could not express
the real producer/consumer gap.  The mechanism class is a
**blocking-contract/nonblocking-consumer mismatch**, not malformed Comfort
bytecode, a bad cursor binding, or the later overlay BRK.

**Track 2 is fully specified and was not executed.**  On the deterministic
B=1/continuation-`$C5B8` reproduction, after tuple/media identity, one

* Bank 0 `$0060..$0061` (`gc_rootsp`) and `$1ECC..$1FCB`

* Bank 0 `$B9E1..$B9E8` (dynamic evaluator holders), `$BD47..$BD59`
  (`lisp_toplevel` `jmp_buf`) and `$BF92..$BF93` (`vm_upvals`);
* physical Bank 5 `$05C680..$05FFFF`, the candidate-derived complete
  namepool/value/name-offset/function-cell domain for `MAX_SYM=752`.

Those ranges cover the dynamic VM continuation and named publication classes

## Broadened holder read complete; retirement holder named — 2026-08-20 (Codex)

Track 2 used the frozen pre-fix world and reproduced the bound stop exactly:
PC `$E096`, SP `$01C5`, MAPL `$0000`, MAPH `$8000`.  One stop and six
raw-first reads captured 15,007 bytes; there was no resume, run, or reset, and
the CPU was left stopped.  The device state is no longer needed.

The holder is now named from live bytes and emitted code.  The active
`lisp_toplevel` `jmp_buf` stores `$C356` at offsets 5/6, the ABI slots for
saved `__rc18/__rc19`.  `$C356` is `defprim`, the first byte of the retired
runtime-overlay window.  The candidate's emitted `setjmp` saves that pair,
`longjmp` restores it, and `__call_indir` is `JMP ($14)`, consuming the same
imaginary-register pair.  The complete causal chain is therefore:

1. `setjmp` snapshots the live `$C356` overlay entry in the top-level
   continuation;
2. the primary `VM_TYPE` abort retires and wipes the overlay;
3. `longjmp` restores the pre-retirement `$C356` snapshot;
4. a later indirect control transfer can re-enter the retired window.

The widened ranges also exclude the other named holder classes.  The live GC
root-stack prefix is empty, `vm_upvals` is NIL, and the evaluator holder cells
contain no aligned runtime-overlay value.  Ninety Bank-5 `symfn` words are
numerically in `$C356..$CA91`, but type decoding classifies all of them as even
`$C000..$DFFE` BCODE immediates, not heap or native pointers.  The publication
area therefore does not compete with the `jmp_buf` finding.

The required liveness contract is now concrete: **before retiring a runtime-
overlay generation, every continuation snapshot that may later be restored
must contain no pointer into that generation.**  A candidate implementation
must cover all seven saved-CSR pairs, every abort exit, preserve all unrelated
setjmp/longjmp ABI state byte-for-byte, and make the deterministic old
reproduction unable to reach the BRK frame.  The machine receipt is
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-stale-holder-broadened-result-receipt.json`;

## Liveness-contract fix card authorized — 2026-08-20 (reviewer)

The broadened read (`507da013`) names the holder exactly:
`lisp_toplevel`'s `jmp_buf` preserves the ABI pair `__rc18/__rc19`
carrying `$C356` — the entry of the retired overlay window — which

other holder class is excluded with typed evidence. The contract follows:
**before retirement completes, no later-restorable continuation may carry
a pointer into the retiring overlay generation.**

**Authorized: exactly one liveness fix card**, with the contract as law
and these requirements:

## Track A corrected at the reviewer's expense; Track B instrument authorized — 2026-08-20

### Track A: the contract is about live control flow, not stored continuations

the finding. The reviewer bound "the restorable-continuation population
derives from the ELF — every setjmp site, every jmp_buf-class store". That
derivation runs and yields **exactly one**: `lisp_toplevel` at `$BD47`.

completeness — a derivation is only as complete as the population it
imagines.

`c2_append_reserve_transient_code_phase`**, executed as `$00` after the
wipe. Nothing stored a stale pointer; **control was still live inside the
retiring generation** and returned into it after the wipe.

The contract therefore sharpens, and this is its permanent form:
**a generation may not be retired while any active frame belongs to it**
— saved continuations are one carrier class among several, and the live
call stack is the other.

**Commissioned, host-only, no device needed:** derive from the final ELF's
control flow every path on which a retirement can execute while a frame of
the retiring generation is live — the abort path being the proven member.
The derivation is static; the unreadable carrier does not block it. Its
output is the enforcement population and a permanent gate: a retirement
reachable with a live in-generation frame falls.

existing ring allocation, 9 E000 code bytes, no ordinary-text and no
symbol price. **The instrument fits inside the owner's existing release**:
59 + 9 = 68 C2-window bytes against the 69 released — no new owner
decision is required.

They are **permanent product code, not a diagnostic build**: this era
retired diagnostic media as a measurement route precisely because a
measurement must happen in the world that ships. A drop counter is also a
standing health signal, not only a one-time probe.

next E000 tenant re-prices or reaches for the relocation lever; the ring
holds **108 usable slots, 14 events of reserve** over the 94-event wall,
which the loss test re-proves on the final world.

## Instrument refuted and re-commissioned; the phenomenon narrows sharply — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The counter reading refutes its own instrument, correctly and early: ring
head and tail are zeroed on Comfort entry, but `seen`/`stored`/`taken` are
never initialized — only incremented. The observed `$00` triple after one
virtual keypress is three stale `$FF` bytes incremented once, not proof of
255 prior events. **An uninitialized instrument measures the world plus its
own history.** The reviewer priced this instrument without asking for its
origin; that gap is the reviewer's, and the rule it buys is permanent:

> **An instrument that carries a claim carries a bound origin.** Its reset
> point is named, atomic, and recorded in the receipt beside the values —
> a counter without a stated zero is telemetry, never evidence.

**What the contact did establish, and it is the sharpest narrowing yet:**
the owner's observation that **the very first slowly typed character (a
plain letter, and `Shift+8`) is already missing**. That is neither ring
overflow nor consumer speed — both are excluded by construction at one
keystroke. The loss sits at the **physical input boundary or its drain**,
and the service-time hypothesis is refuted as the cause of *this*
phenomenon. (The service-time work stands on its own merits — a measured
3.75× improvement — but it was never this bug.)

**Commissioned: one instrument card**, host-only:

1. the three counters are zeroed **atomically at activation**, in the same
   step that resets head and tail; the reset is recorded in the receipt;
2. a **raw-arrival counter** is added *before* any filtering, acceptance
   or normalization, so the next witness measures **physical queue
   arrival against `seen`** — that is the arc no existing counter can see;
3. price against the standing walls (E000 at 6 over floor, far service 37
   free); if the raw counter cannot fit, it returns as a pricing question.

**Candidate hypotheses for the next witness to discriminate — named, not
asserted:** (a) the hardware queue never presents the event; (b) our drain
rejects or skips it; (c) the entry-time head/tail reset discards an
in-flight event or desynchronizes against a concurrently running IRQ;
(d) a correlation with shifted keys (the `Shift+8` observation touches the
shift-table territory of the `$A0` era). The witness decides; none is
adopted as mechanism text.

Device off. No fix before the instrument carries its origin.

The instrument now has one session origin.  Comfort first writes the negative

the next measurement is bounded to fewer than 256 events from this named
Comfort-entry origin.

The fourth counter is `raw` at `$BCFC`.  It advances immediately after

boundaries at `$BCFD..$BCFF`.  The existing 112-byte lifetime allocation is
therefore 108 ring indices plus four counters: **107 usable events**, thirteen
over the 94-event wall, with no new Bank-0 allocation.

The raw `INC abs` grows capture-main from 34 to 37 bytes.  Total instrument

limit; the final form uses the existing `dotimes` lowering, adds no name, and

The machine receipt is
`c2.3-v1.6-input-bound-origin-instrument-receipt.json`.  It records the origin

## Instrument accepted; product link and media released — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The instrument card (`fc47a60f`) carries its origin: an **atomic zero at
Comfort entry** while capture stays closed via `tail=$FF`, four counters
(`raw`, `seen`, `stored`, `taken`), and `raw` measuring `$D60A` queue
presence **before** reading, filtering or ring insertion — the arc no
prior counter could see. Ring at 107 usable slots with 13 events over the
94-wall, no new names, largest Comfort object still 249, full run exit 0.

**Released: the final product link and fresh same-world media**, subject
to the reviewer's independent full run (in flight; media do not pack
before it is green).

**The session's decision table, bound now so the contact needs no
judgement:**

| Comparison | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| pressed > `raw` | the hardware queue never presented it — keyboard/core level |
| `raw` > `seen` | our IRQ read or filter rejected it |
| `seen` > `stored` | the ring write path |
| `stored` > `taken` | the consumer |
| all equal to pressed | nothing is lost — a display or timing phenomenon, not loss |

Session requirements: **stay under 256 events** (the counters are 8-bit),
and the owner's **physical keystroke count is recorded as the reference** —
without it the first arc cannot be decided. Slow single characters,
including a shifted one (`Shift+8`), are the deliberate stimulus.

**Capacity watch, now critical: E000 sits 3 bytes over its floor.** No
further E000 freight without the relocation lever; the far service holds
37 and Bank-2 remains the roomy arena.


## Bound-origin final-link First Red — fragmented by one byte — 2026-08-21 (Codex)

The released successor consumed the full configured world in all 68 real
compiler processes and reached the seed product link.  The linker then stopped
before materialization or final product emission:

> `Comfort input capture main escaped its final-image-derived hole`

This is an honest per-hole capacity Red, not an aggregate-floor failure.  The
candidate map places `input_capture_main` at `$FD08`; the next final-image
tenant begins at `$FD2C`, so that hole contains 36 bytes.  The bound-origin raw
counter grows the indivisible main section from 34 to 37 bytes, ending at
`$FD2D`: **one byte beyond its own hole**.  The second hole remains generous:
`$FEE1..$FF80` contains 159 bytes, of which the 31-byte helper and 70-byte
consumer use 101, leaving 58.

The aggregate arithmetic that predicted three bytes over the 54-byte floor is
still true: 57 bytes remain across both holes.  It was nevertheless
insufficient as a placement proof because free bytes in the second hole cannot
house an indivisible byte from the first tenant.  The permanent lesson is the
fragmentation form of born-derived placement:

> **An aggregate arena reserve does not prove that each indivisible tenant fits
> its own final-image-derived interval.**

The consumed card is sealed in
`c2.3-v1.6-bound-origin-final-card-final-red.json`; it records both intervals,
all three seed section sizes, the generated linker script and the seed map.
Attempt accounting is one WPLTO/product-link attempt, one consumed card, zero
final product artifacts, zero Completion runs, zero media builds and zero
device contacts.  No retry is authorized.  The next decision is a price

placement contract.  Completion and media remain closed.

## Fragmentation finding: aggregate free space is not placement capacity — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The link red (`d336c6b1`) is a genuine placement finding, not behaviour:
`input_capture_main` is 37 bytes and its own hole (`$FD08..$FD2C`) holds
36 — an overflow of **exactly one byte** — while the aggregate free space
still shows 57, three over the 54-byte floor, because the rest lies in the
second hole where an indivisible tenant cannot follow.

**Permanent rule, and it corrects the reviewer's own watch reporting:**

> **Aggregate free space is not placement capacity.** An indivisible
> tenant needs a *hole*, not a sum. Capacity watches report the **largest
> contiguous hole** alongside the total; a surplus-over-floor figure alone
> is a misleading number, and it is the number this desk has been
> publishing.

Every capacity row in this plan and the register is read that way from
here on: E000's meaningful figure is its largest hole, not its 3-byte
surplus.

**Authorized: exactly one card**, either form, whichever the candidate
proves cheaper — the choice is the implementer's:

1. **save at least one byte in `input_capture_main`, semantics
   preserved** — the four arcs, the atomic origin, the raw-before-code-read
   position and the decision table all re-prove unchanged; or
2. **re-cut the placement explicitly** across the existing main/helper
   linkage, with the derived placement gate proving each fragment inside
   its own hole.

Conditions: placement stays born-derived from the final linked image; the
E000 floor is untouched; a form that needs new E000 bytes returns as a
pricing question and reaches for the relocation lever instead of the
floor. Exceptionless; known-family reds self-dispose.

The typo `(requre 'v16core)` produced a clean `UNDEFINED_FUNCTION` and a
correct wipe — and control still reached `$C8B6` in the vacated window,

c2_product_abort_cleanup`) and lay **outside the derived population**,
whose gate covers only the direct overlay abort at `$C939` with a fixed
stack distance. Noted with it: the four input counters read zero because
Comfort was never activated, so this contact says nothing about the
swallowed keys — that measurement stays open.

**Commissioned: the host-only carrier attribution** Codex recommends — the
cleanup/overlay path down to the exact carrier of `$C8B5`. The final ELF
holds exactly one static origin (the legitimate jump at `$C7EB` in the
transient append overlay); how its target survived retirement is not yet

rule already said a derivation is only as complete as the population it
imagines, and imagining carriers has now failed three times. The
attribution therefore also answers:

> Should enforcement stop enumerating **carriers** and instead make
> **retirement safe by construction** — the vacated window itself
> guaranteeing a defined outcome for any surviving transfer, whatever its

Concretely to be priced: today the window is wiped to `$00`, so a stale
transfer becomes BRK and the fail-closed handler catches it — the safety
net already works, but as a session-ending red frame. The question is
whether the BRK handler can recognize **"BRK inside a retired overlay
window"** from the window registry it already owns and recover to the
prompt, turning an ordinary typo from a dead session into a caught error.
That backstop is complete by construction and needs no carrier
enumeration; carrier prevention then remains the best-effort first line,
not the only one.

The attribution prices both: the exact carrier fix, and the backstop, with
their walls and their byte cost. No card, no media, no contact before it
