## Carrier boundary reached; constructive retirement wins — 2026-08-21 (Codex)

The host-only attribution is sealed in
`c2.3-v1.6-retired-window-carrier-inversion-study.json`.  It consumed the
frozen product ELF (`8bb00fd…cd45`) and the authorized raw capture
(`b7cfbffc…c6aa`); it ran zero WPLTOs, links, media builds and device
contacts.

The observed machine fact is exact.  The hardware frame has stacked
`P=$32` (`B=1`) and continuation `$C8B8`, so the executed `$00` BRK begins at
`$C8B6`.  The runtime-overlay interval `$C356..$CA90` is entirely zero and
the registry says `busy=0`, `loaded_len=0`; the outer error remains the
correct `UNDEFINED_FUNCTION`, with phase owner 2 and C2 still ready.  The
corresponding RTS-style carrier value is `$C8B5`.  ElfTruth finds exactly one
static origin of that value: the legitimate `JMP $C8B5` at `$C7EB` in
`.lisp65_rt_c2append_reserve_transient_code`.

The claim boundary matters: `$C8B5` is absent from the stopped stack.  The
dynamic carrier was consumed before BRK created the frame, so the authorized
row cannot name its former storage owner or writer.  Assigning it another
fixed stack offset would be invention.  Consequently an exact
carrier-specific prevention is **not honestly priceable from this evidence**;
proving its writer would require pre-transfer instrumentation across every
dynamic transfer producer — a broader and costlier instrument than the
constructive answer below.  The cleanup ordering also proves why another
one-shot population is the wrong completeness claim: continuation retirement
runs at `$2EA1`, but cleanup then starts the transported abort driver at
`$2EC6`, whose own overlay edge is `$7DDE`.  A pre-wipe census is not the end
of control-transfer creation.

The inversion answer is therefore **yes**.  Carrier prevention stays as the
best-effort first line, but completeness moves to the executed-byte boundary.
Every transfer that actually reaches the zero-filled retired interval becomes
BRK and supplies a carrier-independent hardware frame.  The classifier is:

- stacked `B=1`;
- `(stacked continuation - 2)` in the exact candidate-derived interval
  `[$C356,$CA91)`;
- `rtov_busy=0`, `rtov_loaded_len=0`, and an active top-level landing.

Only that conjunction recovers.  Hardware source-less IRQs, BRKs outside the
window, and genuine BRKs in a live overlay retain the existing fail-closed
policy.  Recovery rewrites the stacked continuation to an always-visible low
landing, preserves an already pending error (or supplies the priced placeholder
`E3e` when none exists), and enters `longjmp` directly — **never** recursively
calling `c2_product_abort_cleanup`.  The existing one-byte RTS stub is not an
arbitrary-transfer landing: it assumes a valid caller frame and is therefore
rejected for this role.

The code price is assembled, not estimated: **69 E000 bytes** for the exact
classifier and **29 ordinary-text bytes** for the cleanup-free landing, 98
bytes gross, zero new state, and zero ordinary-call hot-path tax.  Eight model
cases bind both exact interval edges and the negative domains.  It does not fit
the current candidate: E000 has 57 total free bytes, largest hole 49, and all
57 are already required by the 54-byte floor plus its 3-byte watch; ordinary
text has 14 free.  Floors and margins remain non-budgets.  A successor therefore
needs an explicit relocation/reclaim price in both arenas (or a proved smaller
co-design) before any card can be authorized.

This report answers the architecture question and refuses the unevidenced
half of the carrier question.  No fix, card, media build or device contact is
authorized by it.


## Backstop accepted in principle; three questions before any relocation — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The study (`99328354`) is accepted, including its refusal to name an
unprovable carrier. The inversion is decided: **completeness belongs at
the execution boundary** — a BRK inside a provably retired, zeroed overlay
window returns to the prompt by construction, every other BRK and
source-less IRQ stays fail-closed. That is the right architecture and it
does not depend on imagining carriers.

It does not fit: 69 E000 bytes against a largest hole of 49, 29
ordinary-text bytes against 14 free. **Commissioned, host-only, in this
order — the first question may make the other two unnecessary:**

1. **Is this a regression or a pre-existing product defect?** The abort
   path and overlay retirement are resident product code that shipped in
   v1.5. If a stale transfer into a retired window is reachable there too,
   this is **not** a v1.6 regression: v1.6 may then ship items 1 and 2
   with the behaviour documented in known-issues, and the backstop becomes
   v1.7 freight with its own relocation round — no capacity fight at the
   end of this train. If it is reachable only through v1.6's own overlay
   use, it blocks. Decide from the shipped v1.5 artifacts, host-only.

2. **Can the backstop be smaller?** 69 bytes suggests a full registry
   comparison plus recovery. Price a minimal variant beside it: a single
   in-range PC test against the one known window pair, reusing the
   existing recovery path rather than building one — and the R1 pattern
   (a ~9-byte E000 entry with the body in the far service) as the
   placement shape, noting the far service holds 37.

3. **Only if 1 says "blocks" and 2 does not fit:** the relocation/reclaim
   round, reusing the E000 study's existing per-tenant verdicts rather
   than re-classifying 34 tenants, and stating whether the 1,499-byte far
   arena is itself growable.

Nothing here is urgent by accident: items 1 and 2 remain blocked on the
input-loss measurement regardless, so there is time to answer cheaply
instead of cutting a capacity corner.

interval `$C356..$CA90`, a byte-identical 1,470-byte
`c2_append_reserve_transient_code_phase`, and a byte-identical 29-byte

and 2 of v1.6 are not blocked by this inherited class.  The behavior is

## Release shape decided: measure before reshaping — 2026-08-21 (owner)

The owner asked whether v1.6 should become a pure bug-fix release with
everything else deferred, and accepts the reviewer's answer: **no, not
before the measurement.**

The reasoning is bound so it does not have to be rediscovered:

1. **A bug-fix-only v1.6 would carry no user-visible content.** Every fix
   in this train — the `VM_TYPE` empty phase, PETSCII normalization,
   service time, the partial liveness contract — repairs the new freight
   itself. The one defect that touches the shipped world (the retired
   window) is classified as **not a v1.6 regression** (`2667db6f`) and its
   backstop is v1.7 freight. The remainder (R1 relocation, the checker
   conversions, the instruments) is invisible infrastructure. Dropping
   items 1 and 2 would mean paying the full release process — link, media,
   acceptance contact, Halt A, publication — for nothing a user can see.

2. **The real question is not decidable yet.** The four input counters
   have never run with Comfort active, so it is still unknown whether the
   swallowed keystrokes are ours at all. One short contact decides it
   against the bound decision table: `pressed > raw` puts it at the
   platform boundary (and then likely in v1.5's native REPL too — no v1.6
   blocker); `raw > seen`, `seen > stored` or `stored > taken` each name
   one small, owned arc.

3. **Sequence:** the one-byte fragmentation card, the product link, fresh
   media, one short measuring contact — then the release shape is decided
   on evidence. Items 3 and 4 remain may-slip and are expected to slip, so
   v1.6 is already cut to items 1 and 2; emptying the train further is a
   step to take only if the measurement shows something deep.

## The lost keys are ours: a second consumer on the same queue — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The measurement (`de2ae23a`, 11 > 8 = 8 = 8 = 8) localizes the loss before
our capture path and exonerates ring, consumer and service time. It does
**not** prove the keyboard never produced the events — only that they were
not at `$D60A` when the capture IRQ looked. At slow single-key typing a
hardware queue has no reason to drop 27 %; something took them.

**Source evidence names the taker.** Two readers of the same queue exist:

- `src/optional/c2_kernal_input_capture.s` — the new raster-IRQ capture
  (`lda $d60a` / `lda $d619`, ack by `sta $d619`);
- `src/c2_kernal_window.s:c2_kernal_event_poll` — the pre-existing poll,
  same registers, same acknowledge.

And `src/interrupt.c:143 lisp_poll()` calls the latter in a loop while the
evaluator runs, discarding everything that is not RUN/STOP. Its comment
states the intent plainly: *ordinary keystrokes typed while computation is
running are not editor input and are deliberately drained.*

That was correct before Comfort existed. **The Comfort REPL is Lisp code
running in the evaluator**, so while it waits for input the drain is armed
against exactly the keystrokes the editor is waiting for. Capture at raster
rate usually wins the race; `lisp_poll` sometimes wins, and that keystroke
is gone. This is the single-owner rule violated at a hardware resource: two
owners, one queue.

**Commissioned: the mechanical attribution and its fix, host-only.**

1. **Prove the race rather than argue it** — that `lisp_poll` reaches
   `c2_kernal_event_poll` while Comfort capture is armed, and that its
   drain consumes an event the capture would otherwise have seen. A
   counter on the discard path is the cheap witness if one is needed;
2. **the fix follows the house rule: one owner per resource.** While
   capture is armed (`C2K_INPUT_RING_TAIL` not the closed marker),
   `lisp_poll` must not read or acknowledge the queue at all. RUN/STOP
   keeps its independent matrix authority — the standing wall already
   requires that, so the safety path does not depend on the queue;
3. price it against the walls (ordinary text 14 free, E000 largest hole
   49); the change is expected to be a guard, not freight;
4. every standing wall re-proves, and the loss test gains this case: with
   capture armed and the evaluator polling, **no ordinary event is
   drained**.

This is neither a platform defect nor a regression of the ring: it is an
integration our own freight created and our own instrument found.

## Second-consumer race proved; single ownership implemented — 2026-08-21 (Codex)

The frozen accepted ELF decides the mechanism without another device contact.
It contains the armed raster consumer at `$FD08`, the legacy queue consumer at
`$E000`, and two real calls into the latter; the VM call at `$4776` executes in
the evaluator world that also hosts Comfort. Both consumers read `$D60A`, read
the event at `$D619`, and acknowledge through `$D619`. A one-cell interleaving
is decisive: poll-first leaves capture `raw=0`; capture-first accounts the cell
once. This is the exact shape required by the observed `11 > 8 = 8 = 8 = 8`.

The earlier wording “platform boundary” is therefore narrowed: the contact
located the loss before the product's raw witness, while the linked attribution
identifies the product consumer that can remove it there. No keyboard/core
defect is claimed.

The implementation enforces the resource owner directly in `lisp_poll()`.
When `C2K_INPUT_RING_TAIL != $FF`, the IRQ capture is armed and evaluator
polling returns without reading or acknowledging the hardware queue. Before
returning it still consumes the independent `C2K_BREAK_PENDING` matrix latch,
so RUN/STOP remains a safety authority and never depends on the disputed queue.
When capture is closed, the historical evaluator drain remains unchanged.

The permanent gate carries both schedules and rejects four regressions: absent
ownership guard, absent matrix check, absent matrix acknowledge, or fallthrough
into the queue consumer. The attribution is sealed against the pre-fix Git
world and frozen final ELF; the live gate is born-derived from the current
source. Final-link capacity and final-ELF walls remain the product-card step.

## Single-owner product card First Red: seven ordinary-text bytes — 2026-08-21 (Codex)

The race attribution and semantic fix both passed preflight, including a real
MEGA65 compile of `interrupt.c`. The one authorized card was then consumed and
stopped in its seed link before any candidate ELF/PRG existed:

> `ordinary text displaced the mapped far facade`

The two linker maps make the price exact. The accepted predecessor's `.text`
is `$937F` bytes from `$2023`, ending at `$B3A2` with 14 bytes before the fixed
facade at `$B3B0`. The corrected candidate is `$9394` bytes: **+21 bytes**, an
exclusive end at `$B3B7`, hence **7 bytes of overlap**. The semantic guard is
correct but does not fit; at least seven ordinary-text bytes must be recovered.
The fixed facade, E000 and both resident floors remain untouched and are not
budgets.

This is a genuine capacity/price stop, not a self-disposable verifier class.
No replacement card is authorized. The successor must be priced between a
semantic codegen reduction of at least seven bytes and the established cold
relocation lever; any restructuring must re-prove sole queue ownership and
matrix-owned RUN/STOP at the final ELF.

## Text-capacity red: price the single call site first — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The single-owner fix is confirmed semantically — `lisp_poll` no longer
touches the queue while capture is armed, and RUN/STOP is sourced from the
IRQ's independent matrix latch `C2K_BREAK_PENDING` (`$FF8A`), exactly as
the standing wall requires. The red is capacity only: `.text` grows 21
bytes against 14 free, an overlap of 7. Facade and resident floors stay
untouchable, correctly.

**Priced first, before any relocation: collapse the duplicated call.** The
new form calls `lisp_abort_static(LISP65_ERR_STOPPED, "stopped
(run/stop)")` from **two** sites; each site pays full argument setup plus
`JSR`. A single-exit shape keeps the semantics exactly — armed branch
reads the latch, closed branch drains as before, and both merge into one
call:

```c
uint8_t stop = 0;
if (C2K_INPUT_RING_TAIL != C2K_INPUT_RING_CLOSED) {
    if (C2K_BREAK_PENDING) { C2K_BREAK_PENDING = 0u; stop = 1; }
} else {
    while (c2_kernal_event_poll(&event))
        if (event.code == LISP65_KEY_RUN_STOP) { stop = 1; break; }
}
if (stop) lisp_abort_static(LISP65_ERR_STOPPED, "stopped (run/stop)");
```

Two notes on semantics, both to be proven rather than assumed: the `break`
leaves the drain early where the old form drained on to completion — if
that difference matters, the loop keeps draining and only records `stop`;
and the string literal must remain a single instance, not two.

**Order of routes** for the successor card:

1. the single call site above — measured in emitted bytes, not estimated;
2. if it does not cover the 7, one **cold routine relocates** out of
   ordinary text on the R1 pattern (entry stub plus body in the far
   service, which holds 37) — cold means not on any per-key or per-eval
   hot path, and the card proves that;
3. only if neither works does this return as a pricing question. Floors
   and the facade stay untouched either way.

The independent full run on the settled tree (`c2b8b6a4`, no concurrent

self-dispositional under the standing rules — derive from the candidate,
or rebind era-bound provenance, whichever the record's nature requires.

**Class B — a selftest's deliberate pollution outlives its restore (two
failures):** `v2-workbench-artifacts` and `c2-m65-hw-check` both fail with
`missing defuns/defmacros: %link94-live-symbol-space-noise`. The source is

in a `finally`. The source file is restored — but the artifacts that run
produced under `build/` are not, and later targets consume them.

That is the idempotence class again, one layer deeper than the receipt
rewrites the 2.2 block booked: **a deliberate mutation must be undone in
every state it wrote, not only in the file it edited.** The disposition,
if the mechanism confirms: the noise experiment runs against a
**private copy** — its own suite path and its own output root — so the
shared suite and the shared build tree are never the experiment's canvas.
That is the standing single-owner and process-isolation rule applied to a
selftest.

If the mechanism turns out to be something else, it stops for attribution
rather than a guessed repair; Class B is not self-dispositional until its
mechanism is confirmed.

## Input loss proven gone; the framebuffer is the same class — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

**Milestone, device-proven:** the counters read **8 = 8 = 8 = 8 = 8** and
the swallowed keystrokes are gone. The single-owner queue fix is confirmed
on hardware, insertion is functionally green, and the owner reports the
typing feel as good. The phenomenon that has ridden this seam since the
first contact is closed.

The new first red is its **twin on the other shared resource**: after
`Return`, the correct result `(1 3)` is written through the sequential
screen cursor while the editor's direct-cell writes via `%rl-render` leave
the old input's tail behind — `(1 3) 1 3)` on screen. Two writers, one
framebuffer, different addressing models. And the same gate blind spot as
before: the host gates model the two surfaces **separately** and never
checked the composed image after `Return`.

The rule generalizes the queue lesson to every shared surface:

> **A shared resource has one owner at a time, and a handoff leaves it in
> a defined state.** A gate that models writers separately proves nothing
> about what the user sees; the composed surface is the claim.

**Commissioned: one fix card**, host-only:

1. **preferred form — the relinquishing owner cleans up:** the editor
   leaves its rendered region in a defined state when it hands off at
   `Return` (its own line cleared to end), rather than the result printer
   having to guess how much to overwrite. The card may price the inverse
   (result line takes the whole line) and pick the cheaper, but the
   *defined-state-on-handoff* property is the requirement either way;
2. **the gate proves the composed image**, not the two writers: a
   post-`Return` surface case that fails on any residue — the case this
   red would have caught;
3. Bank-2 placement (the roomy arena; ordinary text at 6 and far service
   at 15 stay untouched), responsiveness unaffected by construction
   (handoff happens at line boundaries), all standing walls re-prove;
4. exceptionless; known-family reds self-dispose.

Items 1 and 2 stay open; no further device contact before the fix is host-
and review-green. The remaining distance is now one visible defect wide.

## Sixth-contact First Red: identity excluded, return/refill split open — 2026-08-22 (Codex)

carrier agrees with the Same-World `%repl-step` payload for every one of the

The primary error is numeric `VM_BAD_BYTECODE` (43).  The surviving VM window
shape uniquely identifies `%repl-step`: header 35, payload 220, payload-window

with either a failed caller payload refill or a completed refill followed by
operand-stack loss at the caller's `DROP`.  The earlier interim claim of a
proved stack loss is explicitly withdrawn.

No fix, link, medium or further device contact is authorized.  The successor
attribution must bind refill success/failure and post-CALL operand depth at
logical PC `$45`; its permanent fixture is the real shape that exposed the
gap: 14 literals, a 21-byte payload window and a nested CALL immediately
before the refill.

## Refill-seam attribution commissioned — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The rescue read (`2e654dfc`) closes the library question cleanly: every
runtime identity is single and consistent, `%rl-screen-tail` returned, and
all 179 captured `%repl-step` payload bytes match the same-world medium.
`VM_BAD_BYTECODE` arises at the **caller-return/refill seam** at payload
PC `$45`, and exactly one binary question remains: **did the caller's
refill fail, or did the operand stack lose a value across a successful
refill?** The read cannot decide it — `WIN_ENSURE` sets the window length
before the actual read, so `$45/21` proves only the *requested* refill,
and `vm_codebuf` lay outside the authorized regions. No mechanism is
claimed; correct.

**Commissioned: a host-only attribution.** The host VM models the window
machinery and the artifacts are the same world, so the seam reproduces at
the desk:

1. drive the host VM through the identical call — `%repl-step` calling
   `%rl-screen-tail` and returning — and **bind refill success and operand
   depth at PC `$45`** as recorded values, both sides persisted;
2. decide the binary mechanically: a failed refill puts the defect in the
   window/refill machinery (the old code-window family, with its standing
   gates as first-class members of whatever follows); a lost operand
   across a successful refill puts it in the split's calling convention —
   the new card's own business;
3. the known third possibility — a refill that succeeds but restores the
   wrong window — is ruled in or out by the same reconstruction;
4. no fix, no card, no link, no media, no device contact before the
   report.

The distance is unchanged: one visible defect, now one binary question
wide.

## Refill-seam attribution result — 2026-08-22 (Codex)

The commissioned host reconstruction runs the exact emitted objects from the
Same-World manifests, not a recompiled approximation.  `%repl-step` executes
its resolved six-argument CALL to `%rl-screen-tail` at `$42`; after the helper
returns, the target-shaped 56-byte window model restores the caller at `$45`.

The recorded seam is unambiguous:

- header 35 bytes, payload 220 bytes, payload-window capacity 21;
- requested and restored window `$45..$59`;
- refill success true and every restored byte equal to the authoritative
  caller payload;
- operand depth immediately before the `$45` DROP: **1**.

Both alternative controls bite.  A forced failed read records refill failure
and no accepted window; a successful read starting at `$46` records unequal
source/destination identities and is rejected as the wrong window.  The host
VM therefore does **not** reproduce the device red: it proves that the emitted
split calling convention preserves the result and that the deterministic
window selection is correct.  Combined with the stopped target's attempted
`$45/21` refill plus `VM_BAD_BYTECODE`, the commissioned binary resolves to a
**real target caller-refill failure**, a member of the standing code-window
family — not the new helper or its calling convention.

No fix is authorized by this report.  The successor must carry the exact
fixture as a permanent first-class gate (255-byte caller, 14 literals,
21-byte window, CALL at `$42`, return/DROP at `$45`) and retain the existing
code-window content/refill gates.  No card, link, medium or device contact runs
before its own disposition.

## State-intersection attribution commissioned — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The refill-seam attribution (`aedc7903`) decides the binary: the host
refill succeeds with a byte-exact restored window and correct operand
depth, the split's calling convention and the wrong-window case are
excluded, and the device red is a **real caller-refill disturbance in the
existing code-window family**. The controls (failed refill, off-by-one
refill) bite correctly.

What separates the two worlds is now the question: the host runs the seam
**without the capture IRQ**; the device runs it with the IRQ armed at
raster rate. That is the obvious suspect space — named as such, not as a
mechanism.

**Commissioned: a host-only state-intersection attribution, from bytes:**

1. derive from the final ELF the complete state the refill path consumes
   and mutates — zero-page ABI cells, MAP/window registers, scratch,
   `vm_codebuf` bookkeeping;
2. derive the complete state the armed IRQ path writes — the 74-byte
   handler, capture, counters, commit;
3. **intersect them.** A non-empty intersection names the corruption
   candidate mechanically (state the IRQ touches while the refill is
   mid-flight), and the fix follows the standing families: save/restore in
   the handler, or ownership separation, whichever prices smaller. An
   empty intersection is a finding too: then the disturbance is not
   register-shaped and the next witness must be an on-device one, priced
   separately;
4. the standing every-exit-unmap and ABI gates are inputs to the
   derivation, not assumptions — if the intersection is empty *because*
   those gates hold, the derivation says so explicitly;
5. no fix, no card, no media, no contact before the report.

## State-intersection attribution result — 2026-08-22 (Codex)

The byte-derived intersection is **empty after the standing ABI,
every-exit-unmap and balanced-stack proofs are applied**.  The final ELF's
owned 74-byte IRQ plus its two capture fragments persistently write only the
VIC acknowledge, keyboard queue acknowledge, frame/episode/break state, the
108-byte input ring and its raw/seen/stored counters.  They write none of the
LLVM-MOS imaginary-register file `$02..$21`, none of `vm_codebuf`, and none of
the `vm_buf_*`/`vmr_*` window bookkeeping.

The apparent machine-state intersection is not a corruption candidate.  The
linked IRQ pushes and restores A/X/Y/Z on every owned return, P and the return
PC are restored by RTI, and its nested capture JSR is balanced below the
interrupted stack pointer.  The IRQ contains no MAP instruction.  Conversely,
the mapped CPU reader brackets its non-baseline MAP interval with PHP/SEI and
restores baseline MAP before PLP, so the IRQ cannot interleave with a mapped
window even in principle.

The resulting classifications are therefore explicit:

- persistent address intersection: **0**;
- ABI-ZP and VM-window intersection: **0**;
- interruptible MAP intersection: **0**;
- CPU registers/stack: shared only under the proved save/restore and balanced
  interrupt-frame protocol.

Consequently neither additional handler save/restore nor ownership splitting
is supported as a fix by this attribution.  The target-only failed refill
remains real but is not register-shaped.  Its next discriminator must be a
separately priced device witness at the actual
`c2_product_entry_read`/caller-refill boundary; this report does not authorize
that witness, a product mutation, a link, media or a device contact.

## Refill-boundary witness pricing commissioned — 2026-08-21 (reviewer)

The intersection is empty with strong evidence (`7dce2e61`): the IRQ
writes nothing the refill consumes, registers and stack are fully saved,
restored and balanced, and the MAP reader blocks IRQs during every
non-baseline mapping. Save/restore work and ownership separation are
therefore **not supported as fixes** — the disturbance is real but not
register-shaped. What remains is time- or content-shaped, and only a
witness at the boundary itself can show it.

**Commissioned: the pricing of a device witness at the
`c2_product_entry_read`/refill boundary**, host-only until priced:

1. **what it records:** per refill, the request (window id, offset,
   length) and the observed result (first/last payload bytes or a cheap
   checksum) into a small bound-origin trace ring — the era's
   find-first-difference discipline applied to the refill seam, so the
   stopped state shows *what the refill actually read* against the
   same-world medium;
2. **bound origin and wrap discipline** per the instrument rule: named
   atomic zero, wrap behaviour stated, both recorded in the receipt;
3. **priced against the thin arenas honestly** — ordinary text 6, far
   service 15, E000 largest hole 49, editor objects near ceiling. The
   ring storage should reuse existing scratch (the `repl.buf` pattern) 
   before claiming any new resident byte; a form that cannot fit returns
   as a pricing question with the relocation lever named;
4. **claim boundary:** the witness diagnoses; it makes no acceptance
   claim, and its removal cost (or its case for staying as product
   health telemetry, like the counters) is part of the price;
5. after pricing and review, **one** short contact reproduces the seam
   red and reads the trace — the reproduction is already deterministic.

No fix before the witness decides whether the refill read wrong bytes,
read right bytes at the wrong time, or read nothing at all.

## Refill-boundary witness pricing result — 2026-08-22 (Codex)

The witness fits without a new resident byte.  `repl.buf` is 192 bytes at
`$BC87..$BD46`; while the native REPL is inactive the live input ring owns only
`$BC90..$BCFF`.  The disjoint prefix and tail therefore provide 80 proven
lifetime-alias bytes.  The priced trace uses 73: a five-byte origin/index
header at `$BC87` and two 34-byte commit-last slots at `$BD00`.  Each slot
records the request (directory/window identity, offset and length), return
status, start/end frame and the exact 21-byte payload.  Seven alias bytes remain
unclaimed.

The origin is an operation, not an assumption: while tracing is closed both
slot commits are invalidated, next/sequence/wrap are zeroed, and `$A5` is
published last as the origin commit.  Slots overwrite oldest, carry uint8
sequence and wrap values, and a contact must stop before 256 wraps.  A torn
last slot is rejected by its missing commit.  This is diagnostic evidence, not
standing product telemetry.

The code prices were emitted as standalone 45GS02 sections, with no product
link:

- first/last bytes: 179 bytes, rejected because a middle-byte corruption has
  identical edges;
- CRC16: rejected here because its 21-byte payload cost includes 168 bit-rounds
  at the very seam whose timing remains open;
- exact payload witness: 205 bytes, selected.

The six free ordinary-text bytes cannot hold that body.  The winning placement
uses the existing relocation lever in reverse: the cold, 211-byte
`vm_runtime_overlay_install_island` body has exactly one incoming edge
(`$A5EA`) and no refill, key or evaluator edge.  A measured nine-byte mapped-far
stub leaves 202 ordinary bytes; the 205-byte witness then leaves three ordinary
bytes free.  The cold body occupies 211 of the separately derived 371-byte
Bank-2 arena `$7E8D..$7FFF`, leaving 160.  Its three external callees
(`$290D`, `$B347`, `$C245`) remain outside mapped CPU block 3.  Existing
Far-service, E000 and resident-island usage do not change.

Availability is an explicit implementation gate.  The sole call in `main`
follows `c2_product_prepare_boot`; the second arena must be materialized as part
of the same Bank-2 static plane before that call.  The implementation must also
prove the nine-byte stub, the complete body range, callee visibility and the
single retargeted WIN_ENSURE read edge from the final linked image.  Pricing
does not inherit those proofs from address arithmetic.

The stopped trace then decides without interpretation:

- no committed result: the refill failed or did not complete;
- committed payload differs from the Same-World source: content-shaped refill
  failure;
- committed payload matches: content is exonerated, and the bound start/end
  frames leave a time-shaped target-only failure.

Retention is dominated.  Removal restores the one read-edge target and the
cold installer's ordinary body, removes the stub/witness/origin writes, and
returns the alias storage without migration.  Unlike the input health counters,
this seam-specific trace has no standing user-health meaning.

Receipt:
`c2.3-v1.6-refill-boundary-witness-pricing.json`.  This result consumed zero
product-source mutations, WPLTOs, product links, media builds and device
contacts.  It prices a successor; it authorizes none.

was this saga's earlier lesson; the medium is one packaging step further,

operation).  Every other size-eligible body transitively reaches another MAP
enter/leave and is rejected by the same graph rule.

Depth counting was emitted beside the winner rather than dismissed by
intuition.  A minimal counter makes enter/leave 32/20 bytes instead of 19/15:
**+18 fixed-facade bytes**, one state byte and about **+23 cycles on every
ordinary outer MAP pair**.  With zero facade padding it does not fit without
another relocation and taxes every hot use thereafter.  It is dominated.

The permanent gate now has a reusable linked-ELF implementation in
`c2_transitive_map_nesting_gate.py`.  Its population is derived from emitted
wrapper body sections, then closed over every sized function tenant in those
sections; no mapped tenant may transitively reach MAP enter or leave.  The
current frozen ELF supplies the positive red control, the proposed population

successor card must run this gate against its own final ELF; direct entry into

not part of the placement successor.

## An unchecked DMA pass-through survived in the product — 2026-08-23 (reviewer)

The follow-up read proves the era's gravest finding: `c2_product_entry_read()`
calls the void DMA load routine and **unconditionally returns success**
(generated `c2_product_runtime.c:1022`), bypassing both the MAP-CPU
transport and the convergence check (`src/vm.c:621`). The dispatch table is
intact (267/267), the VM code window and all refill metadata are fully
null — the null refill is the primary finding, the zero-page jump its
secondary damage. The witness logs only the Comfort target and forwards
every other request into exactly this unchecked path
(`c2_refill_boundary_witness.s:118`).

**Stated plainly: the structural-absence gate's central claim — zero
unsafe DMA readers — was false for the actually linked boot refill.** The
F018B completion-trust class, closed at the root in the 2.1 era, survived
in one generated path. This most likely also resolves the original `$45`
seam red end to end: the host never models DMA timing, the device lives
in it.

**Disposed, in this order:**

1. **Regression question first, host-only, from shipped artifacts:** does
   v1.5.0's boot refill run through the same void-DMA pass-through? The
   answer decides whether the shipped product carries a silent-corruption
   path (then the fix is v1.5-relevant and known-issues/backport enter the
   owner's desk) or whether the pass-through is v1.6-born in the generated
   producer.
2. **Gate blind-spot attribution, host-only:** how did the
   structural-absence gate count zero while this reader exists? The
   suspected shape — the gate scans authored sources while the pass-through
   lives in **generated** product source — is named, not assumed. The gate
   converts to the final-world discipline: content-consuming DMA reads are
   derived **from the linked ELF**, generated code included, with a
   mutation in which exactly this pass-through falls.
3. **The fix card, after 1 and 2:** the boot refill takes the same MAP-CPU
   transport and convergence verification as every other reader — the
   root-fix discipline applied to its last surviving exception. An
   unconditional success return anywhere on a content path falls by
   mutation. **Instrument neutrality is bound with it:** a witness may
   observe a path, never alter its safety properties — non-target requests
   take the same checked path as target ones.

No media, no device contact, no acceptance step before all three land.
The witness has earned its keep twice over; its removal still waits until
the re-proven seam is confirmed.

### Replacement-card Final Red: the generator overwrote the repaired seam — 2026-08-23 (Codex)

The replacement consumed one WPLTO and one product link and then stopped at
`real configurator projection drift`; its binding explicitly requires that
any Red return with the full chain, so no further card is authorized.  The

not the failed conjunct and its two values.  That qualification-mechanics
question therefore remains honestly open until a successor records both
sides; it is not reconstructed by rerunning a consumed card.

The final artifact nevertheless answers the product question independently.
The real profile contains `LISP65_C2_MAP_CPU_TRANSPORT`, and the emitted
candidate's common `c2_source_read` contains the attempted MAP-CPU repair.
But the v6 product generator then **replaces `c2_product_entry_read` in its
entirety** with a template that calls `c2_facade_vm_code_load(2u, ...)` and

edge.  Thus the earlier description "candidate source repaired" named the
wrong seam: it was true of the authored/common helper and false of the
generated boot-refill function that the product actually consumes.

This is the Real-Consumer rule at the source-producer boundary: when a
generator replaces a function, the emitted replacement is the product claim;
repairing the overwritten authored function proves nothing.  The exact writer
is `c2_lite_v6_product_probe.generate_sources`, in its replacement template for
`c2_product_entry_read`.

A successor requires fresh authorization and has two named duties before one
new WPLTO: replace the **generator template** with the MAP-CPU transport and
propagate its returned failure, and run the pre-card gate over the emitted
candidate function itself.  Its final-world gate remains the linked caller
closure: zero unchecked content-DMA readers, a MAP-CPU edge in the real boot
refill, and a neutral witness.  The projection validator must additionally
persist the failed named conjunct and both expected/observed values on any
future drift.  No media or device contact is authorized.

persists the expected set, the actual set and the difference **by name,

## Seam-confirmation First Red: pre-seam Low-RAM BRK — 2026-08-23

The cold contact reached a red frame after the library phase and before any
banner or prompt.  The owner authorized exactly one raw-first stopped-state
read; it consumed one stop, no run, resume, reset or input, and left the CPU
stopped.  The bound product/library readbacks remain byte-identical to their
Same-World media.

The frozen bytes decide the visible layer without deciding its cause:

- the refill witness was never armed: its five-byte origin and both 34-byte
  slots are entirely zero.  This Red therefore precedes the Comfort/refill
  seam and neither qualifies nor condemns the repaired refill;
- the CPU is in the terminal loop of `c2_kernal_fail_closed` at `$E096`, with
  baseline MAP and idle VM/overlay bookkeeping;
- decoding the hardware frame through the emitted IRQ prologue gives saved
  `P=$B1` and continuation `$0607`: this was a software `BRK` at `$0605`;
- `$0605` belongs to no executable section and no sized symbol in the final
  ELF.  It is dynamic Low RAM, not a product instruction boundary;
- the execution-boundary classifier accepts only retired-window continuations
  `$C358..$CA92`.  Rejecting `$0607` was therefore the correct fail-closed
  result, not a false negative;
- `__rc18/__rc19` still contains `$C356`, the historical retired-window entry,
  while the actual BRK is at `$0605`.  Resemblance is not causality: the
  authorized ranges do not contain the current `lisp_toplevel` record,
  `vm_codebuf`, `$0605`'s surrounding bytes or the source-less episode state.

The remaining binary question is consequently narrow: did an earlier accepted
retired-window BRK recover through the live `$C356` continuation and only then
reach `$0605`, or is `$0605` an independent corrupt return/indirect target?
The minimum discriminating read is specified, not executed: the current
19-byte `lisp_toplevel` record at candidate-derived `$BD49`, the 75-byte
`vm_codebuf`/bookkeeping row at candidate-derived `$BFA4`, `$0600..$060F`, and
`$FF83..$FF8D`.  No further device read, fix, build, medium or resume is
authorized by this attribution.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-execution-boundary-first-red-attribution.json`.

## Low-RAM follow-up: history ambiguous, recovery self-rearm proven — 2026-08-24

The owner authorized the four pre-specified rows while the original stop was
still conserved.  The tuple and stack pointer remained byte-identical.  One
stop read 121 bytes, with no resume, run, reset or input; the CPU remains
stopped.

The follow-up corrects its own promised discriminator before drawing a causal
claim.  The current `lisp_toplevel` record still stores `$C356` in saved
`__rc18/__rc19`, while all 75 bytes of `vm_codebuf` and adjacent bookkeeping
are zero.  `$FF86=1`.  Low RAM `$0600..$060F` is
`80 00 00 c0 00 00 e0 00 00 f0 00 00 f8 00 00 fc`; combined with saved
`Y=$BF` and `P=$B1`, this proves that the CPU executed `CPY #$00` at `$0603`
immediately before the observed `BRK` at `$0605`.

Two histories produce every observed byte.  A transfer to `$0601` executes a
first BRK, returns to `$0603`, then reaches the observed second BRK.  Or an
earlier source-less episode — possibly an accepted retired-window BRK — leaves
the episode byte at one before a transfer to `$0603`.  No existing byte records
the accepted branch or its original PC.  `$FF86` is episode state, not history;
the commissioned read was therefore insufficient to select between the two.

It nevertheless proves a product defect independently of that history.  The
accepted boundary path redirects to `retired_window_resume`, which tail-jumps
directly to `longjmp(lisp_toplevel)`.  The live record's offsets 5/6 contain
`$C356`, and emitted `longjmp` restores them to `__rc18/__rc19`; no sanitizer
exists on that edge.  Whenever the backstop accepts this stale-jmp_buf member,
its recovery deterministically restores the carrier it was meant to contain.
The boundary catches dead-window execution, but its recovery state is not yet
safe.

The required contract is now exact: before `longjmp` on an accepted boundary
path, the target continuation contains no in-generation saved CSR pair;
recovery may not restore its triggering carrier.  This evidence does **not**
claim that the self-rearm caused this exact `$0603` transfer, and it authorizes
no fix, build, medium, resume or additional contact.

Evidence:
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/architecture-blocks/c2.3-v1.6-execution-boundary-followup-result.json`.

The follow-up also exposed and closed one evidence-only live-plan binding: the
preceding attribution checker now reads its plan bytes from its sealing commit
`63bac8b1` rather than from the growing live plan.  Its receipt remains
byte-identical; historical evidence once again checks its own world.

## Recovery-sanitization seam First Red attributed — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The contact stopped immediately after the libraries with a red frame.  One
owner-authorized raw-first read captured registers, stack, VM/boot state and
the refill witness, then left the CPU stopped without reset, resume or input.
The witness origin and both slots are entirely zero: Comfort never armed it,
so this is an early-boot failure and neither qualifies nor condemns the former
Comfort refill seam.

The frozen stack is an actual boot-banner call: `main → repl → vm_run_dir →
vm_run`.  The packed `%repl-banner` object itself executes green in the host VM
for 8,693 steps with the expected NIL result and no type error.  The target
state instead records `VM_TYPEERROR` followed by a software BRK at `$004C`;
that low-RAM transfer is a downstream observation, not the attributed root.

The root is decided by the final linked bytes.  Generated source declares
`c2_facade_map_cpu_read` with the assembler identity
`c2_facade_runtime_overlay_exec`, and the old final-ELF gate counted that call
as a MAP-CPU edge.  The fixed vector at `$B5EB`, however, jumps through a
return-sensitive selector.  The complete final caller population is:

- `c2_product_entry_read`: `JSR $B5EB` at `$A109`, hardware-pushed return
  `$A10B`;
- `c2_stream_c2d_read`: call at `$E326`, pushed return `$E328`;
- `c2_stream_shelf_read`: call at `$E84B`, pushed return `$E84D`.

The selector admits only `$E328` and `$E84D` to `c2_map_cpu_read` at `$22E5`.
Every other identity takes its historical fallback to
`vm_runtime_overlay_exec` at `$2A25`.  Thus the new boot reader's `$A10B`
identity deterministically selects the wrong transport: the source binding is
present but the real linked consumer never consumes it.  This is the familiar
**bound ≠ consumed** family in its path-sensitive return-selector layer, not a
new device or DMA-timing hypothesis.

The two blind spots are explicit.  The final DMA closure counted an edge to
the facade without executing its selector; the local-return gate proved two
registered identities without deriving every caller of the vector.  The
permanent successor rule is therefore: derive the complete final-ELF caller
population of every return-sensitive vector and execute its selection on each
actual hardware-pushed return identity.  The zero-unsafe-DMA claim must consume
that result; registration alone is never consumption.

No fix is authorized by this attribution.  The preferred price candidate is
structural: because `c2_product_entry_read` is ordinary text, call
`c2_map_cpu_read` directly and leave the fixed selector to its two fixed-window
callers.  Admitting the third identity is the comparison option.  Either form
must change the upstream generator, not merely its materialized output, and
carry the all-caller selector gate into the final DMA closure.  Accounting:
zero WPLTOs, links, cards, media builds or additional device contacts.

   address is not an identity — every analysis and gate that keys on
   addresses inside the MAP arena carries the mapping domain.* The
   attribution inventories which existing gates are address-only in that
   arena, so the ambiguity class dies once, not per gate.

## Selector-bypass media blank-screen First Red attributed — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The authorized raw-first capture and its five-byte follow-up conserve one
stopped CPU and decide the visible failure mechanically.  This is not a slow
boot, render-only blank or fail-closed frame.  The CPU is in a two-edge
infinite control cycle:

- `$2020: JSR $8040` pushes return `$2022`;
- entering `$8040` interprets the operand byte of the valid `$803F: BRA $8063`
  as `JSR ($0884)`; the blank screen supplies `$20 $20` at `$0884/$0885`, so
  this edge returns to `$2020` and pushes `$8042`;
- the complete 256-byte hardware stack is consequently the alternating
  pattern `80 22 20 42` repeated 64 times.  The pre-cycle frames are gone.

The follow-up refutes the first content hypothesis: live `$808C..$808E` is
still exactly `4C 41 80` (`JMP $8041`).  The legitimate loop-back was not
changed to `$8040`.  ElfTruth also says `$8040` is neither an instruction
boundary nor a linked direct `JMP`/`JSR` target.  The free native trampoline
`__call_indir` cannot be the immediate ingress: it still exposes `$C356` in
`__rc18/__rc19`, and neither edge of the terminal cycle modifies that pair.
The refill witness was never armed, so this contact does not qualify or
condemn the repaired seam.

The first ingress remains open rather than guessed.  Its surviving classes
are a live jump-table head differing from the final ELF, a corrupted RTS/RTI
continuation, or a live-mutated direct transfer outside the five-byte read.
The original stack cannot name the predecessor because the cycle overwrote
it completely.

The next minimal discriminator is specified but not authorized: with captured
`X=0`, read the first two bytes of each of the thirteen linked indirect tables
(26 bytes total) and compare them with their candidate-derived targets.  Any
live `$8040` head names the computed-transfer source; thirteen byte-identical
heads exclude the whole table-dispatch class and send the next price to a
pre-wrap RTS/RTI/direct-edge witness.  If the device has been powered off, the
same read follows one deterministic reproduction.  No fix, build, medium,
resume or further device read is authorized by this attribution.

## Selector-bypass blank-screen table discriminator — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The owner-authorized 26-byte read ran against the conserved original stop,
after a tuple preflight proved `$PC=$2020`, `$SP=$011E`, `X=0` and baseline
MAP unchanged.  It introduced no second stop, resume, reset, run or input and
left the CPU stopped.

All thirteen live X=0 table heads are byte-identical to their candidate-ELF
values: `$4DB9`, `$6770`, `$742E`, `$6A10`, `$70DF`, `$803D`, `$843D`,
`$8DBD`, `$9517`, `$C7E5`, `$C43D`, `$C39D`, `$C49A`.  None targets the
non-boundary `$8040`.  The entire indirect table-dispatch class is therefore
mechanically excluded, not merely made unlikely.

The first ingress remains open in two classes only: a corrupt RTS/RTI
continuation whose predecessor the terminal cycle erased, or a live-mutated
direct transfer outside the authorized reads.  The next instrument to price
is consequently a pre-wrap control-edge witness: it must record the final
non-cycle transfer into `$8040` before the `$2020↔$8040` cycle overwrites the
hardware stack.  This result authorizes no fix, build, medium or further
device read.

## Pre-wrap control-edge witness pricing released — 2026-08-24 (reviewer)

The 26-byte read (`23f44d4a`) excludes the indirect table-dispatch class
completely — all 13 heads byte-identical to the final ELF, none pointing
at `$8040`. Two candidates remain, both visible only **before** the cycle
overwrites the stack: a corrupt RTS/RTI continuation, or a live-modified
direct control transfer.

**Released: the pricing of a pre-wrap control-edge witness**, host-only
until priced, under the full instrument law this saga has bound:

1. **what it captures:** the last control transfer into the `$8040`
   region — source PC, transfer kind (RTS/RTI vs direct), and the stack
   top at that instant — latched **once** before the cycle can wrap the
   stack (a first-event latch, not a ring: the first arrival is the whole
   question);
2. **instrument law applies in full:** bound atomic origin; **instrument
   neutrality** — the witness must not alter the timing or semantics of
   the path it observes, and the pricing proves that rather than asserts
   it; removal after attribution is the bound default;
3. **priced against the current arenas honestly** (ordinary text 18, far
   service 11, MAP arena 47 — domain-aware placement per the new rule) and
   reusing existing scratch before claiming any new byte;
4. after pricing and review: **one** reproduction contact reads the latch
   at the stop — the reproduction chain has been deterministic throughout.

The candidates are named, the mechanism is not guessed; the latch decides.

## First-event witness priced — diagnostic-core winner, no product freight — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The pricing found a complete zero-product-byte form, but also rejected the
obvious incomplete version before calling it ready.  The stock monitor cannot
carry the claim for two independent reasons:

- its `z` history reader exposes only `$000..$2FF` of the 1,024-entry
  monitor-private history RAM (`CMP #3`), while a continuously wrapping event
  can occupy any slot;
- an address-only breakpoint is ambiguous here.  The legitimate
  `$803F: BRA $8063` advances PC through `$8041` while reading its operand at
  `$8040`, so PC alone cannot distinguish operand fetch from execution that
  begins at `$8040`.

The priced winner is a volatile diagnostic-core successor using only existing
monitor resources:

1. the stopped-state reader changes `CMP #3` to `CMP #4`; emitted bytes prove
   one changed immediate byte and zero code-size delta;
2. the existing breakpoint comparator is armed at `$8041` and qualified by
   CPU decode state `$13/$14`.  This rejects the legitimate Cycle2 operand
   fetch and catches normal as well as fast-dispatch entry at `$8040`.  It adds
   no register, BRAM, product byte or product cycle; final LUT use and timing
   remain synthesis acceptance conditions;
3. continuous history records one 24-byte row per target-CPU microcycle in
   monitor-private dual-port BRAM.  The unique decode row at PC `$8041` is the
   first target fetch.  Walking backward modulo the circular history to the
   prior decode row derives the exact source PC as `PC-1`; that row's read byte
   is the source opcode and distinguishes RTS/RTI from direct transfer.  The
   target row supplies first-event SP; the eight bytes above that SP remain
   intact even if the target performs a later downward push.

The decoder is host-proved for RTS, 6502 RTI, absolute JMP and fast-dispatch
JSR, including a history wrap.  A synthetic legitimate `$803F` operand-fetch
row with PC `$8041` is rejected because its state is Cycle2, not decode.
Seven mutations reject an incomplete aperture, any product byte, a missing
stack field, asserted rather than proved neutrality, a dropped state
qualifier, a false readiness claim and a spent device contact.

The price against the candidate-derived arenas is therefore exactly zero:
ordinary text remains 18 free bytes, Far Service 11 and MAP diagnostic 47;
product state and scratch both remain unchanged.  Origin is the final `tl`
publication while the target is held after stale history/break state has been
cleared.  Removal is the default: reload the stock `a915893` core after the
single attribution contact; no product or medium removal exists.

This pricing does **not** authorize materialization or contact.  The local
workspace has no complete core synthesis/toolchain path (no Vivado/updatemem
and no complete Ophis checkout), and the decode-state qualifier is RTL rather
than a BRAM-only patch.  The next authority, if accepted, is one host-only
diagnostic-core materialization/provenance step with synthesis/timing proof;
the volatile-load reproduction remains a separate owner contact after that.
No product source, WPLTO, link, bitstream, medium or device was touched.

## The witness lives in the core; the toolchain question goes to the owner — 2026-08-24 (reviewer)

The first-event latch pricing (`7d3bcc33`) found the ideal witness shape:
**zero product bytes, zero scratch, arenas untouched** — one monitor-ROM
byte (`CMP #3 → CMP #4`, size-neutral) plus a state-qualified breakpoint
(PC=`$8041` AND decode state `$13/$14`), the qualification proven
necessary because the legitimate `BRA $803F` also touches `$8041` during
operand fetch. Seven mutations fall. Instrument neutrality is perfect by
construction: the observed machine is unmodified at the product level.

The cost moved instead into **infrastructure**: materializing the
diagnostic core needs synthesis and timing proof, and the local machine
lacks Vivado / the full core toolchain.

**Ordered questions, the cheap one first:**

1. **Can the one-byte monitor change plus breakpoint land by bitstream
   patching instead of synthesis?** BRAM-init patching (updatemem-class
   tooling, or the MEGA65 project's own core utilities) can alter
   initialized ROM contents in an existing bitstream without Vivado
   synthesis; whether the breakpoint qualification also fits that route
   (existing debug/monitor hooks vs. new logic) is exactly the host-only
   question — answered from the core sources and tools already on disk or
   freely fetchable, no install commitment;
2. **if synthesis is unavoidable: the owner decides the toolchain
   install.** Sizeable one-time cost (Vivado), but with standing strategic
   value beyond this witness — the parked Attic/L10 family and every
   future core-level question (the register's own "on every new stable
   core release" trigger) become first-class workable instead of
   upstream-blocked;
3. **the fallback without core tooling is named honestly:** repeated
   reproduction with the existing raw-first reads gives statistical
   discrimination between the two candidates at best, not proof — weaker,
   and priced only if 1 and 2 both fail the owner's judgement.

No core build, no flash, no device contact before the owner's word on the
route.

## Bitstream-patch question decided — the complete witness needs synthesis — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The cheap route is closed, for two independent and source-proved reasons.

First, the one monitor byte is content-shaped but this exact core does not
provide a supported post-implementation path to it.  The bound build graph is
`monitor.a65 -> bin/monitor.m65 -> monitor_mem.v -> Vivado synthesis / implementation
-> bitstream`: `mempacker_v` emits Verilog, not memory-map metadata.  The tracked
core contains no `.mmi`/`.bmm`, `updatemem`/`data2mem` target or equivalent
bitstream-memory writer.  `bitinfo` only inspects configuration packets,
`bit2mcs` packages an existing bitstream, and `hotpatch` translates a 64-KiB
target-CPU memory context.  None edits monitor BRAM in a routed bitstream.

The apparent `MONITOR.M65` escape hatch is unrelated: it is an SD-card Freezer
utility, distinct from `bin/monitor.m65`.  Nor can the embedded monitor rewrite
its own code through the runtime bus: only monitor-private `$0000-$01ff` is
writable, while the code starts at `$f200` and the `$f000-$ffff` decode is
read-only.

Second — and decisively even if a valid BRAM-init patcher were supplied — the
required breakpoint is not content.  The stock predicate is exactly
`monitor_break_addr == monitor_pc && monitor_break_en`.  CPU decode state reaches
the controller, but is consumed only by recent-state/history recording; no
monitor register or existing hook expresses `PC=$8041 AND state=$13/$14`.
Address break, flag break and watch are independent triggers, not a programmable
conjunction.  Adding the qualifier changes LUT/interconnect logic.  BRAM-init
patching cannot do that, and blind routed-bit rewriting without implementation
metadata would satisfy neither timing nor instrument-neutrality proof.

Therefore Question 1 is answered **no for the complete witness**.  The monitor
byte alone is neither supported by the bound packaging nor sufficient; synthesis,
implementation and timing proof are unavoidable for the state qualifier.  Seven
mutations reject an invented patch route, an invented runtime loader, an invented
state hook, a content-only completeness claim, a no-synthesis claim, a spent core
build and a spent contact.

This was attribution only.  No toolchain was installed, no core or product source
was changed, no core was built, no bitstream was written or loaded, and no device
was contacted.  Route 1 is closed.  The next decision is the owner's Question 2:
whether the Vivado core toolchain is worth the one-time infrastructure cost.  The
statistical fallback remains unpriced and weaker.

## THE FINISH PLAN — owner directive 2026-08-24

Owner premise, binding: **we do not debug the MEGA65 core.** We work
within the given hardware and available software. No Vivado, no core
builds, no bitstream work — the core route is **closed**, not parked
(question 2 is thereby answered: neither option). No further excursions;
the goal is a deliverable v1.6.

### Step 0 — housekeeping (implementer, hours)
Register `c2_v160_boot_refill_selector_bypass_media.py` in the
media-builder enumeration (catalogue, self-dispositional). Nothing else.

### Step 1 — the clean product candidate (implementer, ~1 day)
One world containing **only product freight and product fixes**:
items 1 and 2 (cursor navigation; Comfort REPL with hybrid input, `l65>`
prompt, display ownership), the input counters (product telemetry,
proven), and the real fixes: DMA generator fix, queue single-owner,
normalization, empty phase, facade/packed-PRG media proof, nested-MAP
ban, backstop with sanitized recovery.
**Removed: all diagnostic freight** — the refill-boundary witness, its
MAP-arena installer, every latch plan. Rule, permanent: *diagnostic
freight lives in diagnostic worlds; acceptance runs on the product
world.* Full `check-source` green on this world; enumeration rows for
removed tenants convert once.

### Step 2 — media and ONE acceptance session (owner, 1 contact)
Fresh media under the standing gates. The acceptance session for items 1
and 2: prompt rows, abort row (backstop expected to recover to prompt),
input rows including fast typing, composed display. Bound meanings:
- **green → items 1 and 2 are accepted; Halt A follows immediately;**
- an `$8040`-like early-boot red on the *clean* world means the cycle is
  real product: then **emulator diagnosis (xemu)** — available software,
  within the premise — never the core;
- any other new red: one rescue read, then triage (Step 3). 

### Step 3 — triage rule (the anti-rabbit-hole clause, binding)
Every finding from Step 2 gets exactly one classification:
- **blocks daily use of items 1/2** → at most **one** fix round; if a
  second round would be needed, the affected feature descopes to v1.7
  instead;
- **rare or cosmetic** → known-issues entry plus v1.7 row. 
**No new instruments, no new walls, no raised bars.** The existing bars
stand; none are added.

### Step 4 — Halt A and the release ladder (owner)
Halt A decides on the session result as measured. Items 3 and 4 slip to
v1.7 as long expected. Then the standard ladder reused from v1.5: D5
headroom row **with repl-comfort loaded** (machine-readable in the release
contract, as bound), release media, publication.

### Budget and abort line
Target: **about one week, two to three device sessions total.** Hard
abort: if the clean candidate needs more than two fix rounds to pass
acceptance, v1.6 descopes on the spot (ship item 1 alone, or convert
v1.6 to a fixes-and-foundation release) — the owner decides which, on
numbers, not on hope.

The `$8040` file, the witness tooling and the core pricing remain sealed
evidence — closed chapters unless the clean world reopens them.

## Step-2 First Red — nested result lifetime across caller refill — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The clean product booted, both libraries returned `t`, and the first `(repl)`
failed with `vm: bad bytecode`.  The single authorized read preserved the
actual product world.  It proved the runtime symbol publications single and
coherent, the complete `%repl-step` Bank-4 payload byte-equal to its canonical
object, and the live `vm_codebuf` window `$45..$59` byte-equal to that payload.
At logical `$45` the byte was the canonical `DROP`; media, loader, object,
refill destination and runtime identity are therefore exonerated.  The earlier
host attribution that inferred a failed target refill from a capture without
`vm_codebuf` does not describe this clean-world instance.

The remaining product seam was the VM's nested-call result lifetime.  Both
`OP_CALL` and `OP_CALLPRIM` kept `res` only in a compiler-local temporary while
`BUF_ENSURE_MINE(pcur)` repaired the caller's shared code window.  The repair is
a real product read and may cross service seams; the sole live object reference
was not a GC root during it.  The one Finish-Plan fix round moves `PUSH(res)`
before that repair.  Error exits remain on the common `done` path, which restores
`gc_rootsp` to its entry base.  The final emitted bytes prove the root-stack
write precedes the caller-window ownership tests and any refill call for both
opcodes.

The single replacement card is green: one WPLTO, one product link, one Scope
and one Acceptance; diagnostic freight remains absent.  Its immutable pair is
ELF `b390ec5d…` / PRG `2dbd282e…`.  Artifact-only packaging then consumed that
pair with zero WPLTOs, links or cards.  The replacement product medium is
`V16B.D81`, SHA-256 `f716c471…`; `V16BLIB.D81` remains byte-identical to the
unchanged library medium at `5b1a9f58…`.  The 19-artifact closure, Same-World
build ID `0x0401e53e`, 98-byte packed-PRG facade and full mapped Static Plane
all pass.  This is the already-budgeted single product fix round, not a raised
bar or a new instrument; the next device session repeats the original four
Step-2 groups.

## Item-1 device First Red and sole fix round — 2026-08-24 (Codex)

The first Item-1 contact reached the native prompt and loaded `v16core`, but
the first printable byte after `(read-line)` produced `vm: type error`.  The
mechanism is closed at the desk: `%rl-put` unconditionally called private
`key-event` mode 3 after the first byte, while the deliberately Comfort-free
product exposes only public modes 0/1.  The cursor fixture had hidden the
fault by enabling private modes even for its public `read-line` cases.

The first priced repair made mode 3 conditional at run time.  It was rejected
before promotion: although the public lane passed, the added branch reduced
the already-paid Comfort service margin from about 29% to 3.3%.  A v1.6 fix
must not spend v1.7's measured responsiveness evidence.

The winning form is therefore a feature-derived compiler projection.  The
canonical source remains the fast Comfort-capable implementation.  The
Item-1 `v16core` configurator projects a public source with modes 2/3 absent,
and its real compiler produces an artifact in which `%rl-render` and
`%rl-put` contain zero `key-event` calls while `%read-line-loop` contains
exactly the public mode-1 call.  A third execution lane runs the complete
cursor suite with private modes disabled, and a mutation restoring mode 3
falls.  The canonical Hybrid suite separately retains its 29% service margin.

The sole R1 product link was already consumed before this library-seam choice;
the product pair is unchanged.  The replacement medium is artifact-only and
binds the public projection, emitted disassembly and manifest beside the
unchanged R1 pair.  The rejected run-time-guard medium is never promoted.

The resulting public medium is `V16I1Q.D81` at
`bb1d8c382da435da9d7f17eb5cfe1bd77568a781b1b7d9283293904532713395`;
its one-row library medium is `V16I1QL.D81` at
`caa08cc36931cc758c0a1fd68102b7042263c39d982bed5b60145ea3b4b9208b`.
The bound product pair remains PRG `4d80051c80473e26f3a8b4582d8e0200ec9d15e5e6faa4e1cd7984e6a97b4f6c`
and ELF `82bc474e61a0ba4691abe52c3f0c8fcf6e26335f533df2d59ddd3ab2f3eba489`.

recovery and ordinary evaluation after the error.  No red frame or hang
occurred anywhere in the session.

Therefore **v1.6 Item 1 is hardware-accepted and Halt A is reached under the
owner's Item-1-only disposition.**  The claim does not include Comfort,
lossless fast input, D5, release acceptance, or Items 2–4.  The next rung is
the bound D5 headroom measurement without `repl-comfort`, followed by the
release ladder.

## D5 green — candidate seal open — 2026-08-25 (Codex)

The owner ran the five physical D5 forms on a fresh boot of the accepted

medium and emitted bytes exclude `repl-comfort`.  The observed rows were
`v16-perf-probe`, `0 2`, `0 (9 2)`, `0 98`, and `0 42`.

After every row had returned, the authorized runner took its sole stop and
saved one 49,152-byte physical Bank-0 capture.  Its first host-side verifier
invocation rejected the relative capture pathname after the bytes were
already safely on disk.  No device action was repeated: a read-only resume
fed the same capture to the same verifier by absolute workspace path.  The
separate First-Red receipt preserves that adapter failure.

ElfTruth derived `nsym` at `$005F` and `npool` at `$BE16` from the accepted
candidate ELF.  The stopped device held 647 symbols and 8,795 name bytes,
leaving **105 symbol slots and 1,413 name bytes** against the mandatory
32/384 floors.  Therefore D5 is green for the shipped configuration and the
candidate-seal rung is open.  No WPLTO, link, media build, FTP action or
second stop occurred.

## SEALED — 2026-08-25 (post-v1.6 housekeeping block)

v1.6.0 is published from public `main` at `9938c274`, annotated tag
`v1.6.0`; all four release assets passed fresh-download verification. The
machine authority is
`tests/bytecode/dialect-v2/evidence/post-release/v160-public-publication-receipt-20260825.json`.
This plan is now historical: it describes the worlds and reds that produced
the release, not a live source of configuration.

The finish plan did exactly what its bounded form promised. Posten 1 — cursor
navigation — passed the reset bar and shipped. **Posten 2 moved intact to
v1.7** after the clean-product candidate reproduced the sealed `(repl)`
bad-bytecode failure twice; capture, hybrid service, prompt and display
ownership were removed together rather than partially accepted. Posten 3 and
Posten 4 slipped. The reset acceptance bar was navigation correctness plus no
regression against v1.5; it made no lossless-input, Comfort, Attic-write or
sub-30-second-boot claim.

Five independent fixes survived the descope and shipped with Posten 1:
verified MAP-CPU boot refill, the packed-facade media proof, the
execution-boundary backstop with sanitized recovery, hardware-queue
single-owner enforcement, and the transitive MAP-nesting prohibition. The
selected public world holds 105 free symbol slots and 1,413 name bytes. All
future work starts from the parked-items register and the lean v1.7 pre-plan,
never by appending to this file.
